| Item | Reference |
|---|---|
| Work order system | ⬜ [INSERT WORK ORDER SYSTEM NAME] |
| Submission link | **[Work Order Submission Link]** |
| Mobile access | ⬜ [INSERT — confirm mobile-friendly submission; required for DSPs without desk computers] |
| Site roster | Section 2.3 |
| Asset inventory | SOP-11 |
| Intake form template | Appendix A |
| Escalation contacts | ⬜ [INSERT ESCALATION CONTACT SHEET LINK] |

## G. Definitions
| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Work order / ticket** | A logged request with a unique number. |
| **Priority** | How fast we respond, based on impact and urgency combined. |
| **First response** | The first human acknowledgement with a named owner — not an automated receipt. |
| **Resolution** | The problem is fixed and the requester has confirmed it. |
| **Recurring issue** | Three or more tickets on the same asset, site, or issue type within 90 days. |
| **Workaround** | A temporary measure that restores service without fixing the root cause. A ticket is not closed on a workaround; it is downgraded and stays open. |

## H. Roles and Responsibilities
| Role | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| **Requester** | Submit through the link; provide complete information; be available for contact; confirm resolution |
| **Supervisor / Site Lead** | Ensure staff know the process; assess operational impact; escalate when service delivery is threatened; verify recurring problems are flagged |
| **IT / Facilities triage** | Acknowledge within SLA; set correct priority; assign; communicate; escalate; close only after requester confirmation |
| **Assigned technician / vendor** | Update the ticket at each meaningful step; document root cause; record parts, labor, and cost |
| **Director of IT / Director of Facilities & Fleet** | Manage queue health; own aging and breach reporting; run recurring-issue analysis |
| **COO** | Review dashboard monthly; approve SLA targets; resolve cross-department priority conflicts |

## I. Procedure

### I.1 — How to submit a work order (staff-facing, 8 steps)
1. **Confirm** it is not an emergency. **If** there is a fire, a medical emergency, a gas smell, flooding, an active threat, or immediate danger, **then** call **911** first and notify your supervisor and [Emergency Contact Number]. Submit the work order afterward.
2. **Try** the safe first-step troubleshooting for your issue type (SOP-04 printers, SOP-05 phones, SOP-06 internet). Two minutes of checking often resolves it.
3. **Open** **[Work Order Submission Link]**.
4. **Complete** every required field (I.2). Incomplete tickets are the leading cause of slow resolution.
5. **Attach** a photo of any error message, damaged equipment, or asset tag. A photo of the screen is worth three sentences of description.
6. **Select** the urgency honestly (I.3). Everything marked emergency means nothing is.
7. **Submit** and **record your ticket number**.
8. **If** your issue is Emergency or High priority, **then** also **call [IT Support Phone]** (or [Emergency IT Contact] after hours) and give the ticket number. Do not rely on the ticket alone for urgent issues.

### I.2 — Required form fields

| Field | Required | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| Requester name | ✅ | Ownership and callback |
| Job title / department | ✅ | Routing and access questions |
| **Site / property** (from Section 2.3 roster) | ✅ | The join key for all reporting |
| Specific location at site (room, office, unit, vehicle) | ✅ | Technician arrives at the right place |
| Best contact phone | ✅ | Must be a number answered now, not a desk phone that is broken |
| Alternate contact (supervisor or covering staff) | ✅ | 24/7 sites change shift |
| Email | ✅ | Ticket updates |
| **Issue category** | ✅ | Routes to the right queue (I.2.1) |
| **Asset / equipment involved** (asset tag, serial, vehicle number, room device) | ✅ where applicable | Builds asset history for lifecycle planning |
| **Issue description** | ✅ | See I.6 for good examples |
| Exact error message text | ✅ if one exists | Cuts diagnosis time dramatically |
| When it started / how often | ✅ | Distinguishes new failure from chronic |
| Who else is affected | ✅ | Drives priority |
| **Urgency** (Emergency / High / Normal / Low) | ✅ | See I.3 |
| **Operational impact** (what cannot be done) | ✅ | The real priority driver |
| Does this affect resident services, medication documentation, or safety? | ✅ Yes/No | Immediate escalation flag |
| Photos / attachments | Optional but encouraged | |
| **Preferred access time** and access instructions | ✅ | 24/7 sites need coordination; some rooms are locked |
| Any access restrictions (resident privacy, quiet hours, program schedule) | ✅ | Prevents disruptive visits |
| Prior ticket number, if related | Optional | Links recurring issues |

**I.2.1 — Issue categories** ⬜ *(confirm against the chosen system's configuration)*
`IT – Account/Access` · `IT – Email` · `IT – Computer/Laptop` · `IT – Printer` · `IT – Phone/VoIP` · `IT – Internet/Network` · `IT – Software/Application` · `IT – Mobile Device` · `IT – Security Concern` · `IT – New Equipment Request` · `Facilities – HVAC` · `Facilities – Plumbing` · `Facilities – Electrical` · `Facilities – Structural/Doors/Windows` · `Facilities – Appliance` · `Facilities – Grounds/Snow` · `Facilities – Life Safety Equipment` · `Facilities – Accessibility/Adaptive Equipment` · `Fleet – Vehicle Mechanical` · `Fleet – Vehicle Body/Damage` · `Fleet – Vehicle Lift/Accessibility Equipment` · `Fleet – Scheduled Maintenance` · `Security – Doors/Locks/Badges/Cameras` · `Other`

### I.3 — Severity and priority matrix

**Priority = Impact × Urgency.** Triage sets the final priority; the requester's selection is an input, not the decision.

| | **Urgency: Now** | **Urgency: Today** | **Urgency: This week** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Impact: Resident safety / whole site / whole organization** | **EMERGENCY (P1)** | **HIGH (P2)** | **HIGH (P2)** |
| **Impact: Multiple staff / a program function** | **HIGH (P2)** | **NORMAL (P3)** | **NORMAL (P3)** |
| **Impact: One staff member, work blocked** | **NORMAL (P3)** | **NORMAL (P3)** | **LOW (P4)** |
| **Impact: One staff member, workaround exists** | **NORMAL (P3)** | **LOW (P4)** | **LOW (P4)** |

| Priority | Definition | Proposed first response | Proposed resolution | Who is notified |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **P1 — EMERGENCY** | Resident safety or health at risk; a site cannot operate; organization-wide outage; active security incident; no ability to call out for emergency help | **15 minutes, 24/7** | **4 hours** or documented workaround with continuous updates | Director of IT/Facilities, COO, VP of Operations, site leadership, and CEO if >4 hours |
| **P2 — HIGH** | A program function is down; multiple staff blocked; a critical deadline at risk (payroll, billing cutoff, survey); single-site internet or phone outage; a vehicle out of service affecting transport | **1 business hour** | **1 business day** | Director of IT/Facilities, site supervisor |
| **P3 — NORMAL** | One person blocked; degraded but functioning; routine requests | **4 business hours** | **3 business days** | Assigned technician |
| **P4 — LOW** | Convenience, cosmetic, future-dated requests, enhancements | **1 business day** | **10 business days** or scheduled | Assigned technician |

*All targets above are **proposed and require leadership approval**, and must be reconciled with the MSP contract (SOP-16) before publication. Do not publish an internal SLA the MSP contract does not support.*

### I.4 — Examples by priority

**P1 — EMERGENCY (call, don't just submit)**
1. No phone service at a group home — staff cannot call 911 or the on-call nurse.
2. Internet down at a site where medication administration or clinical documentation is electronic.
3. Suspected ransomware: files renamed, ransom note on screen, mass file changes.
4. Loss of heat in winter or cooling in extreme heat at a residential site.
5. Water leak affecting electrical equipment or resident living space.
6. Fire alarm, sprinkler, or emergency call system failure.
7. Wheelchair lift failure on a vehicle with a scheduled transport.
8. Door lock or access control failure leaving a site unsecured.
9. Complete loss of access to the clinical documentation system organization-wide.
10. Confirmed exposure of resident or employee data.

**P2 — HIGH**
1. A site's internet is down but paper downtime procedures are working.
2. The shared program computer will not start and it is the only one at the site.
3. Payroll system inaccessible on a payroll processing day.
4. A billing user cannot access the billing system near a submission deadline.
5. The main office printer is down and checks or required notices must go out today.
6. A vehicle is out of service and a day program route is affected.
7. Multiple staff report they cannot receive email.
8. A badge reader fails at a main entrance during business hours.
9. A refrigerator/freezer failure where food storage is affected.
10. Phone system voicemail down organization-wide.

**P3 — NORMAL**
1. One laptop is running very slowly.
2. A desk phone display is blank but calls work through a softphone.
3. A user needs software installed from the approved list.
4. A monitor has failed and a spare is available.
5. A leaking faucet.
6. A printer produces streaked output.
7. A new employee needs a mailbox and access (submit at least 5 business days ahead — see SOP-09).
8. A vehicle needs routine scheduled maintenance.
9. A door closer is slow but the door secures.
10. A conference room display will not connect.

**P4 — LOW**
1. Request to relocate a printer.
2. Request for a second monitor.
3. Cosmetic wall repair.
4. Request for an additional label printer.
5. Future-dated setup for an event.
6. Preference change in an application's configuration.

### I.5 — When to call, text, or dial 911 instead of waiting

> ### ☎️ DO NOT WAIT FOR A WORK ORDER
>
> **Call 911 immediately for:** fire · smoke · gas odor · medical emergency · active threat or violence · a person in immediate danger · significant flooding with electrical hazard · structural collapse.
>
> **Call [IT Support Phone] / [Emergency IT Contact] immediately (and submit a ticket after) for:**
> 1. Suspected ransomware, malware, or hacking — see SOP-08.
> 2. You clicked a phishing link or entered your password on a suspicious page.
> 3. A lost or stolen laptop, phone, or tablet with organizational data.
> 4. Complete phone outage at a residential site.
> 5. Complete internet outage at any site.
> 6. Any incident where resident or employee information may have been exposed.
> 7. Complete loss of a system needed for medication documentation or clinical records.
>
> **Call [Emergency Contact Number] (program on-call) for:** any situation affecting resident safety, supervision, or care that cannot wait.
>
> **Rule of thumb:** *If someone could be harmed, or the organization could lose data, pick up the phone. The ticket is the record, not the alarm.*

### I.6 — Sample work order descriptions

**❌ Weak:** "Printer broken."
**✅ Strong — Printer:**
> Site: [Site ID – River Vale Admin, 2nd floor copy room]. Asset tag: IT-PRN-0142. The printer shows "Paper Jam – Area 3" on the display. I opened the front tray and removed one crumpled sheet, closed it, and the same message returned. No paper is visible in Area 3. Affects 6 staff. We have a required mailing that must go out today by 4:00 PM. Photo of the error screen attached. Access: open 8–4, ask for [Name] at the front desk. Priority: High.

**✅ Strong — Phone:**
> Site: [Site ID – Group Home 7]. Device: desk phone, kitchen wall, extension 412. No dial tone since approximately 6:00 AM today. The screen is lit and shows the extension. I checked that the network cable is seated at the phone and it is. Staff cell coverage at this site is poor. This is the phone we use to reach the on-call nurse. No other working landline at this site. Priority: EMERGENCY. I also called [Emergency IT Contact] at 6:15 AM.

**✅ Strong — Internet:**
> Site: [Site ID – Day Program 3]. All staff lost internet at approximately 10:20 AM. Wi-Fi shows connected but no pages load. Wired computer in the office also fails. The modem/router lights are: power solid green, internet light amber/blinking. I did not change any settings. 14 staff affected. Attendance and documentation cannot be entered; we are on paper downtime forms. Cell service works at this site. Priority: HIGH. Photo of equipment lights attached.

**✅ Strong — Account access:**
> User: [Name], Program Coordinator, [Site ID]. System: clinical documentation system. Error: "Your account has been locked." Started this morning at 7:05 AM after three sign-in attempts. I have verified Caps Lock is off. I have an iPad that may have an old saved password. I need access to complete documentation due by end of shift at 3:00 PM. Best callback: [mobile number]. Priority: HIGH. *(Note: password resets require a phone call — see SOP-02.)*

**✅ Strong — Cybersecurity (submit AND call):**
> I received an email at 9:12 AM appearing to be from [Executive] asking me to purchase gift cards urgently and reply with the codes. The reply-to address is different from our domain. I did not reply or click anything. I reported it with the Gmail "Report phishing" button and forwarded it to [phishing address]. I called [IT Support Phone] at 9:15 AM. Screenshot attached. Priority: HIGH. *(If you had clicked or replied: EMERGENCY, call immediately.)*

**✅ Strong — Facilities:**
> Site: [Site ID – Group Home 3]. Location: main bathroom. The hot water is not working; cold water is normal. Started yesterday evening. Water heater is in the basement utility room; I have not touched it. 5 residents are affected and morning routines are disrupted. Access: staff on site 24/7, please call [number] before arrival. Priority: HIGH.

**✅ Strong — Fleet:**
> Vehicle: Unit 214, plate [XXX]. The wheelchair lift stops halfway and beeps three times. It started this morning. The vehicle is currently at [Site ID]. This vehicle is assigned to the [Day Program] route at 8:00 AM tomorrow; two riders require the lift. No alternate accessible vehicle is available at this site. Priority: EMERGENCY (transport for tomorrow at risk). Photo of the lift control panel attached.

### I.7 — Assignment, acknowledgement, and tracking
1. **Triage** every new ticket within the first-response SLA and assign a named owner. An unassigned ticket has no owner and will age.
2. **Confirm or correct** the requester's priority. **If** priority is changed, **then** record the reason in the ticket and notify the requester.
3. **Acknowledge** to the requester with: ticket number, assigned owner, confirmed priority, and expected next update time.
4. **Update** the ticket at every meaningful step. For P1, update at least every 30 minutes; for P2, at least daily; for P3/P4, at least every 3 business days.
5. **If** a ticket will breach its resolution target, **then** notify the requester and the queue owner **before** the breach, with a revised estimate and the reason.
6. **If** the ticket requires a vendor, **then** record the vendor, the vendor reference number, the promised date, and follow up on that date. The organization's ticket stays open until the organization's problem is fixed — a vendor ticket is not a closure.
7. **If** the requester is unreachable for 3 business days after two documented attempts, **then** place the ticket in "Pending Requester" and close after ⬜ [recommend 5 additional business days] with notice to the requester and supervisor.

### I.8 — Closure and satisfaction confirmation
1. **Verify** the fix with the requester before closing. **Never** close a P1 or P2 without direct confirmation from the requester or site lead.
2. **Record** the root cause in a standardized field — not just "resolved." "Rebooted" is not a root cause.
3. **Record** parts used, labor time, vendor cost, and any asset changes (SOP-11).
4. **If** the fix is a workaround, **then** do not close; downgrade priority and keep the ticket open with a documented plan and target date.
5. **Send** a one-question satisfaction survey on closure ⬜ *(recommend a simple 1–5 with an optional comment; target response rate 30%)*.
6. **If** the requester reports the issue is not resolved within 5 business days of closure, **then** reopen the original ticket rather than creating a new one, so the recurrence is visible in reporting.

### I.9 — Recurring problems and asset history
1. **Flag** any asset, site, or issue type with **3+ tickets in 90 days** as a Recurring Issue.
2. **Open** a Recurring Issue review record containing: ticket history, total labor and parts cost to date, asset age, warranty status, vendor involved, and root-cause hypothesis.
3. **Decide** and document one of: repair with root-cause fix · replace the asset · change the vendor · change the process · accept and monitor with a review date.
4. **Route** decisions with a cost above ⬜ [INSERT THRESHOLD, e.g. $2,500] to [Chief Operating Officer]; above ⬜ [INSERT] to [Executive Director/CEO].
5. **Feed** every recurring-issue decision into the lifecycle replacement plan (SOP-22) and the vendor scorecard (SOP-16).
6. **Report** the top 10 recurring issues monthly in the executive dashboard.

> **The mechanism, stated plainly:** if we fix the same thing three times, the fourth occurrence is a design failure, not a maintenance event. The Recurring Issue flag exists to force that conversation on a schedule instead of leaving it to whoever notices.

### I.10 — Escalation paths

| Situation | Escalate to | When |
|---|---|---|
| P1 not acknowledged in 15 minutes | Call [IT Support Phone], then [Emergency IT Contact], then [Director of IT] | Immediately |
| P1 unresolved at 4 hours | [Chief Operating Officer] | At 4 hours |
| P1 unresolved at 8 hours, or affecting resident safety | [Executive Director/CEO] + [VP of Operations] | At 8 hours |
| P2 unresolved at 1 business day | Queue owner (Director of IT or Director of Facilities & Fleet) | At breach |
| Vendor missing a committed date | Vendor account manager, then [Director of Finance] for contract remedies | Same day |
| Landlord/property-management responsibility | [Director of Facilities & Fleet Management] → property manager, in writing | Same day |
| Disagreement about priority | Site supervisor → queue owner → COO | Same day |
| Any ticket where resident safety is at risk | [VP of Operations] + on-call management chain, immediately, by phone | Immediately |

### I.11 — "How to Submit a Good Work Order" checklist

> **☐ GOOD WORK ORDER CHECKLIST**
> 1. ☐ Is this an emergency? If yes — **call first**, then submit.
> 2. ☐ Did I try the two-minute safe checks (SOP-04/05/06)?
> 3. ☐ Did I enter the **site** and the **exact location**?
> 4. ☐ Did I include the **asset tag, serial, extension, or vehicle number**?
> 5. ☐ Did I type the **exact error message**?
> 6. ☐ Did I say **when it started** and **whether it happened before**?
> 7. ☐ Did I say **who else is affected** and **what work is blocked**?
> 8. ☐ Did I say whether **resident services or safety** are affected?
> 9. ☐ Did I attach a **photo**?
> 10. ☐ Did I give a **phone number that will actually be answered**?
> 11. ☐ Did I give **access times and instructions**?
> 12. ☐ Did I write down my **ticket number**?

## J. Escalation Criteria
See I.10. Additionally: any ticket aged beyond twice its resolution target is automatically escalated to the queue owner; any P1 is automatically visible to [Chief Operating Officer].

## K. Required Documentation and Record Retention
| Document | Retention |
|---|---|
| Work order records (all fields, updates, closure, cost) | 7 years |
| Recurring-issue review records | 7 years |
| Vendor dispatch records and invoices tied to tickets | 7 years |
| Satisfaction survey results | 3 years |
| Monthly queue and SLA reports | 3 years |
| P1 incident records | 7 years (and cross-referenced to SOP-17 if a major incident) |

## L. Service-Level Target
See the priority table in I.3. *All targets are proposed and require leadership approval and MSP contract reconciliation.*

## M. Quality-Control Checklist
- ☐ Submission link is live and tested from mobile, desktop, and a shared site workstation
- ☐ Printed contact card posted at every site in the Section 2.3 roster
- ☐ 100% of tickets have a site from the official roster (no free-text sites)
- ☐ ≥90% of tickets have a complete impact statement
- ☐ Zero P1 tickets closed without requester confirmation
- ☐ Root cause recorded on ≥95% of closed tickets
- ☐ Recurring-issue flags reviewed monthly
- ☐ Aging report reviewed weekly by queue owners
- ☐ No parallel intake channels in active use (audited quarterly — if staff are still emailing individuals, the process has failed, not the staff)

## N. Related Policies and Forms
SOP-04 · SOP-05 · SOP-06 · SOP-08 · SOP-11 · SOP-16 · SOP-17 · SOP-22 · Appendix A · QR-1 · QR-2

## O. Revision Notes
| Version | Date | Change |
|---|---|---|
| 1.0 | ⬜ | Initial issue; unified IT/Facilities/Fleet intake adopted |

# SOP-04 — PRINTER TROUBLESHOOTING AND PRINTING STANDARDS

**B. SOP Number:** ITG-SOP-04 | **Version:** 1.0 | **Effective:** ⬜ [DATE]

## C. Purpose
1. Resolve the majority of printing problems at the desk in under five minutes, without a technician visit.
2. Prevent injury and equipment damage from unauthorized repair attempts.
3. Protect confidential documents left in output trays — one of the most common and most preventable privacy exposures in a multi-site organization.
4. Control printing and supply costs and identify devices that should be replaced rather than repeatedly serviced.

## D. Scope
All organization-owned or leased printers, copiers, multifunction devices, label printers, and scanners at all sites; and all printing from organizational systems.

## E. Owner
**Accountable:** [Chief Operating Officer] · **Responsible:** [Director of Information Technology] (device/network) and [Director of Facilities & Fleet Management] (physical placement, power, supply logistics) · **Consulted:** Compliance (confidential output)

## F. Required Systems, Tools, Forms, or Links
[Work Order Submission Link] · [IT Support Phone] · asset inventory (SOP-11) · print vendor ⬜ [INSERT VENDOR + SERVICE CONTRACT NUMBER] · supply ordering process ⬜ [INSERT] · Appendix A

## G. Definitions
| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **MFD** | Multifunction device: print, copy, scan, sometimes fax. |
| **Print queue** | The list of jobs waiting to print on your computer. |
| **Secure print / PIN release** | A feature that holds a job until you enter a code at the device. |
| **Consumables** | Toner, drums, waste containers, staples, paper. |

## H. Roles and Responsibilities
| Role | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| **Employee** | Perform the safe checks in I.1; collect confidential output immediately; report faults with the asset tag; never attempt internal repair |
| **Site/office lead** | Maintain local paper and toner stock; monitor unclaimed output; ensure the device location supports confidentiality |
| **IT** | Maintain drivers, queues, network connectivity, security settings, and device inventory; coordinate the print vendor |
| **Facilities** | Power, placement, physical security, and removal/disposal logistics |
| **Print vendor** | Hardware service under contract; supply fulfillment where contracted |
| **Compliance** | Standards for handling and disposing of printed PHI |

## I. Procedure

### I.1 — Safe first-step troubleshooting (staff, in order)
1. **Read** the message on the printer's display and write it down exactly. Take a photo.
2. **Check power:** Is the display lit? **If not, then** confirm the power switch is on and the power cord is seated at both ends. **If still dark, then** try a different known-working outlet on the same circuit — **if** that fails, **then** submit a work order (category: IT – Printer; note possible electrical issue).
3. **Check paper:** Open the tray, confirm paper is present, squared, below the fill line, and not damp or curled. Fan the stack and reload. Close the tray fully until it clicks.
4. **Check toner:** Read the display for a low/empty toner message. **If** toner is low, **then** replace it per I.4 **if** you have been trained and a spare cartridge is on site; **otherwise** submit a work order.
5. **Check the connection without changing anything:** confirm the network cable is seated at the back of the printer and at the wall jack, or that the printer shows as connected on its display. **Do not** change any network settings, IP address, or Wi-Fi configuration.
6. **Check your print queue:** open the printer queue on your computer, cancel any stuck or duplicate jobs, then try one small test page. A backlog of failed jobs blocks everything behind it.
7. **Check which printer you selected:** confirm the printer name in the print dialog matches the device in front of you. Printing to the wrong device is the most common "printer not working" report — and when the document is confidential, it is also a privacy incident (see I.3.6).
8. **Restart the device safely:** power the printer off using its power button, wait 30 seconds, power it back on, and wait for it to fully initialize (2–3 minutes on an MFD). **Do not** unplug a device mid-job or during startup.
9. **Restart your computer** if the problem is only on your computer and other people can print to the same device.
10. **If** the issue persists after these steps, **then** submit a work order with everything you observed.

### I.2 — Prohibited actions
1. **Do not** open internal panels, remove fusers, or reach into the interior beyond the normal paper path and toner access doors. Fuser units operate at high temperature and cause burns.
2. **Do not** use tools, force, or sharp objects to remove jammed paper.
3. **Do not** attempt repairs on leased or contract-serviced equipment — it voids service coverage and may trigger a chargeback.
4. **Do not** refill toner cartridges or install non-approved consumables.
5. **Do not** change network settings, IP addresses, or Wi-Fi configuration on any device.
6. **Do not** install printer drivers or software downloaded from the internet. Drivers are deployed by IT.
7. **Do not** connect a personal printer to the organizational network or to an organization-owned computer without IT approval (SOP-13).
8. **Do not** move a printer or MFD without a work order — they are heavy, they are inventoried assets, and they require a network drop.
9. **Do not** scan documents containing PHI to a personal email address or an unapproved destination.

### I.3 — Confidential printing and pickup
1. **Print confidential material only to a device you can reach immediately.** Never send a confidential job to a printer on another floor, in another building, or at another site.
2. **Stand by the printer** for anything containing PHI, personnel data, financial account data, or donor data.
3. **Use secure/PIN release** where available ⬜ *[INSERT which devices support it — recommend enabling on all MFDs in shared areas; this is a low-cost, high-value control]*.
4. **Collect** output immediately. Never leave documents in the tray "to get later."
5. **Check** the tray for other people's documents when you collect yours. **If** you find unclaimed confidential output, **then** deliver it to the owner or your supervisor, or place it in the locked shredding container — never in a recycling bin or open tray.
6. **If** confidential material printed to the wrong device or the wrong site, **then**: retrieve or secure it immediately; notify your supervisor; report to [IT Support Email] and Compliance within **1 hour**; complete Appendix E. **If** it cannot be retrieved, **then** it is a potential privacy incident and Compliance determines the response.
7. **Dispose** of printed confidential material only in locked shredding containers or by cross-cut shredder. Never in a trash or recycling bin. See SOP-08.18.
8. **Do not** print resident information at a home workstation or personal printer.
9. **Print the minimum.** If the record exists in the system, ask whether a printed copy is needed at all.

### I.4 — Paper jams and toner replacement
**Paper jam:**
1. **Read** the display for the jam location.
2. **Open** only the doors and trays the display or the device's printed diagram indicates.
3. **Pull** paper slowly and evenly **in the direction of paper travel**. Never pull backward against the rollers.
4. **Remove** every piece, including small torn fragments. A remaining fragment causes an immediate re-jam.
5. **Close** all doors and trays firmly.
6. **If** the jam message persists after two attempts, **then** stop and submit a work order. Repeated attempts damage rollers.
7. **If** you cannot see or reach the paper, **then** stop. Submit a work order.

**Toner replacement** *(only if trained and a spare is on site)*:
1. **Confirm** the cartridge model matches the device.
2. **Open** the toner access door.
3. **Remove** the used cartridge as shown on the device's diagram.
4. **Shake** the new cartridge gently side to side if the instructions direct it.
5. **Remove** the seal or tape from the new cartridge.
6. **Insert** until it seats and clicks. Never force it.
7. **Close** the door and confirm the display clears.
8. **Place** the used cartridge in the return/recycling packaging and follow ⬜ [INSERT TONER RECYCLING PROCESS].
9. **Wash** your hands if toner contacts skin; use cold water only.
10. **If** toner spills, **then** do not vacuum with a standard vacuum; notify Facilities.
11. **Update** local stock count and reorder if below the minimum (I.6).

### I.5 — When to submit a work order vs. escalate immediately

| Submit a work order (P3 Normal) | Submit AND call [IT Support Phone] |
|---|---|
| Streaks, lines, faded or smeared output | Burning smell, smoke, or sparking — **unplug and call, and call 911 if there is smoke or fire** |
| Repeated jams after safe steps | Any electrical shock or exposed wiring |
| Device offline after a restart | The only printer at a site is down and required documents must go out today (P2) |
| Slow printing | A confidential document printed to the wrong location and cannot be retrieved (also notify Compliance) |
| Scanner not sending to email | Payroll, check, or regulatory deadline at risk today (P2) |
| Toner needed with no spare on site | A device was tampered with, or an unknown USB device is attached to it |
| Noisy operation | Suspected data exposure from a device's stored memory |

### I.6 — Supplies and inventory
1. **Maintain** a minimum on-site stock: ⬜ [recommend 1 spare toner per installed cartridge color, 1 case of paper per high-volume device].
2. **Assign** a named supply owner per site; record in the site roster.
3. **Reorder** when stock reaches the minimum — not when it runs out.
4. **Order** through ⬜ [INSERT SUPPLY ORDERING PROCESS/VENDOR] only. Individual purchasing of consumables is prohibited (it defeats contract pricing and breaks cost reporting).
5. **Record** consumable consumption by device. A device consuming toner disproportionately to its page count is either failing or being used for work that belongs elsewhere.
6. **Review** print volume and cost per site quarterly. **If** a device's annual service plus consumable cost exceeds ⬜ [recommend 50%] of replacement cost, **then** open a lifecycle replacement review (SOP-22).

### I.7 — Recurring printer failures
1. **If** a device generates 3+ tickets in 90 days, **then** it is flagged under SOP-03 I.9.
2. **Compile** the device's ticket history, age, page count, service cost, and warranty/lease status.
3. **Decide** and document: repair, replace, relocate, or retire.
4. **If** replacing, **then** confirm secure disposal of the device's internal storage (SOP-11.7) — MFDs retain images of scanned and printed documents, and an unwiped device leaving the building is a data breach.

### I.8 — One-page quick-reference flow

```
PRINTER NOT WORKING?
│
├─ Is there smoke, burning smell, or sparks?
│     └─ YES → Unplug if safe. Call 911 if fire/smoke. Call [IT Support Phone]. STOP.
│
├─ Is the display dark?
│     ├─ YES → Check power switch → check cord both ends → try another outlet
│     │          └─ Still dark → SUBMIT WORK ORDER (note: possible electrical)
│     └─ NO  → continue
│
├─ Is there an error message on screen?
│     ├─ PAPER JAM  → Follow SOP-04 I.4. Two attempts max → WORK ORDER
│     ├─ OUT OF PAPER → Reload, squared, below fill line, close fully
│     ├─ TONER LOW/EMPTY → Replace if trained + spare on site → else WORK ORDER
│     └─ OTHER → Photograph it → WORK ORDER with exact text
│
├─ Nothing printing at all?
│     ├─ Did I select the RIGHT printer?  → Check the name in the print dialog
│     ├─ Is the print queue jammed?        → Cancel stuck jobs → print 1 test page
│     ├─ Is the network cable seated?      → Reseat only. CHANGE NOTHING ELSE.
│     └─ Restart printer (off, 30 sec, on, wait 3 min) → then restart my computer
│
├─ Still broken?
│     └─ SUBMIT WORK ORDER at [Work Order Submission Link]
│        Include: site · asset tag · exact error · what I already tried · photo ·
│                 who is affected · deadline at risk · access time
│
└─ Is a deadline today at risk, or is this the only printer at the site?
      └─ YES → Mark HIGH and CALL [IT Support Phone] with the ticket number
```

## J. Escalation Criteria
| Trigger | Escalate to | Timeframe |
|---|---|---|
| Smoke, burning smell, sparking, shock | 911 if fire; [IT Support Phone] + Facilities | Immediately |
| Confidential output to wrong location, unretrievable | Supervisor → IT → Compliance | 1 hour |
| Only printer at a site down with a same-day requirement | [IT Support Phone], priority High | Immediately |
| 3+ tickets on one device in 90 days | Director of IT (recurring-issue review) | At third ticket |
| Device tampering or unknown attached hardware | [IT Support Phone] — treat as security incident | Immediately |

## K. Required Documentation and Record Retention
Work orders (7 years) · device service history and cost (life of asset + 7 years) · consumable consumption by device (3 years) · secure disposal certificates for MFD storage media (7 years) · confidential-output incident reports (6 years, Compliance).

## L. Service-Level Target *(proposed, requiring leadership approval)*
| Situation | First response | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| Single printer down, alternative available | 4 business hours | 3 business days |
| Only device at a site down | 1 business hour | 1 business day |
| Deadline-critical (payroll, regulatory, checks) | 1 business hour | Same business day or documented alternative |
| Toner delivery to site | — | 2 business days |
| Vendor on-site hardware service | Per contract ⬜ [INSERT] | Per contract ⬜ [INSERT] |

## M. Quality-Control Checklist
- ☐ Every device has an asset tag visible to staff and matches the inventory
- ☐ Secure/PIN release enabled on all shared-area MFDs, or an exception is registered
- ☐ Locked shredding containers present at every site that prints confidential material
- ☐ No printer is placed where output is visible to visitors or individuals served
- ☐ Site supply owners named in the site roster
- ☐ Quarterly print cost-per-site review completed
- ☐ Disposal certificates on file for every retired MFD

## N. Related Policies and Forms
SOP-03 · SOP-08 · SOP-11 · SOP-14 · SOP-19 · SOP-22 · Appendix A, E

## O. Revision Notes
| 1.0 | ⬜ | Initial issue |
|---|---|---|

# SOP-05 — DESK PHONE AND VoIP TROUBLESHOOTING

**B. SOP Number:** ITG-SOP-05 | **Version:** 1.0 | **Effective:** ⬜ [DATE]

## C. Purpose
1. Restore telephone service quickly, because in a residential program a phone is a life-safety device, not an office convenience.
2. Give staff safe steps that do not risk misconfiguring the phone system.
3. Ensure every site always has a documented way to reach emergency services and on-call clinical support.
4. Control who can change extensions, forwarding, and call routing.

> ### ⚠️ LIFE-SAFETY NOTICE
> **Every site must have a working method to call 911 at all times.** VoIP phones depend on internet and power. **If** the phones at a site are down, **then** the site supervisor must immediately confirm and announce the backup method (staff cell phones, a designated cell phone, or a neighboring site), notify the on-call manager, and log it. ⬜ **[INSERT the designated backup calling method for each site in the Section 2.3 roster — required before adoption.]**
>
> **E911 address accuracy:** VoIP calls to 911 route based on a registered address. **Any phone that is physically moved to a different site or room must have its registered emergency address updated by IT before use.** Moving a VoIP phone without notifying IT can send emergency responders to the wrong building.

## D. Scope
All desk phones, conference phones, softphones, voicemail, call groups, auto-attendants, and organization-issued cell phones used as primary site contact numbers.

## E. Owner
**Accountable:** [Chief Operating Officer] · **Responsible:** [Director of Information Technology] · **Vendor:** ⬜ [INSERT VoIP PROVIDER + SUPPORT NUMBER + ACCOUNT NUMBER]

## F. Required Systems, Tools, Forms, or Links
[Work Order Submission Link] · [IT Support Phone] · [Emergency IT Contact] · VoIP provider portal (IT only) · site roster (Section 2.3) · extension directory ⬜ [INSERT LINK]

## G. Definitions
| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **VoIP** | Telephone service over the internet. If the internet is down, VoIP phones usually stop working. |
| **Extension** | Your internal number. |
| **Call group / hunt group / ring group** | One number that rings several phones. |
| **Auto-attendant** | The recorded menu that answers the main number. |
| **Softphone** | A phone app on a computer or mobile device. |
| **PoE** | Power over Ethernet — many desk phones draw power from the network cable, not a wall adapter. |
| **E911 address** | The physical address registered with the phone service for emergency calls. |

## H. Roles and Responsibilities
| Role | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| **Employee** | Perform the safe checks in I.1–I.2; record a professional voicemail greeting; report outages immediately; never move a phone between rooms or sites without a work order |
| **Site supervisor** | Confirm and announce the backup calling method during an outage; verify emergency numbers are posted; log the outage; ensure coverage of the site's main line |
| **IT** | All system configuration: extensions, call groups, forwarding, voicemail access, permissions, E911 addresses; vendor coordination |
| **VoIP vendor** | Service availability, carrier issues, hardware replacement per contract |

## I. Procedure

### I.1 — Safe physical checks (change nothing)
1. **Look** at the phone's screen. Is it lit? What does it say?
2. **If** the screen is dark, **then**:
   a. Confirm the network cable is firmly seated in the port marked for the network (usually labeled with a network icon), and in the wall jack.
   b. **If** the phone uses a power adapter, confirm it is plugged in at both ends and the outlet works.
   c. **Do not** swap cables between ports on the back of the phone — one port feeds the phone, one passes through to a computer, and swapping them takes both offline.
3. **If** the screen shows an error, a "registering," "no service," or "network" message, **then** note the exact text and take a photo.
4. **Check** whether other phones at the site are also affected. **If** several are, **then** this is likely a site network or service issue — go to SOP-06 and report it as a site outage.
5. **Check** whether the internet is working at the site. **If** the internet is down, **then** the phones will be down; report it as one combined incident, not two tickets.

### I.2 — Safe restart
1. **Hang up** any active calls.
2. **Unplug** the network cable (or power adapter) from the back of the phone.
3. **Wait** 30 seconds.
4. **Reconnect** the cable firmly.
5. **Wait** up to 3 minutes for the phone to fully restart and register. Do not interrupt it.
6. **Test** by calling another internal extension.
7. **If** the phone still does not work, **then** submit a work order and, if the site has no other working phone, **call [IT Support Phone] immediately**.

### I.3 — Symptom-specific steps

| Symptom | Staff steps | If not resolved |
|---|---|---|
| **No dial tone** | 1. Check screen is lit. 2. Confirm handset is seated properly in its cradle and the curly cord is plugged into both the handset and the phone base. 3. Try the speakerphone button — if speakerphone has dial tone, the handset or its cord is the fault. 4. Safe restart (I.2). | Work order; **call if it is the only phone at the site** |
| **Cannot receive calls** | 1. Check the screen for a forwarding icon or a "Do Not Disturb"/DND indicator and turn DND off. 2. Confirm you are not signed out of the phone. 3. Ask a colleague to call your extension and then your direct number — note which fails. 4. Check voicemail: are calls going straight to voicemail? | Work order; note whether internal, external, or both fail |
| **Cannot make calls** | 1. Note the exact error or recording. 2. Test an internal extension, then a local number, then a long-distance number — note which fail. 3. Confirm you are dialing any required prefix ⬜ [INSERT DIALING PLAN, e.g. 9 for outside line, 10-digit local]. | Work order with the exact failure pattern |
| **Poor audio: choppy, echo, robotic, one-way** | 1. Test with the handset, then speakerphone, then headset — note which are affected. 2. Note the time of day and whether it happens on all calls or specific ones. 3. Report whether the site's internet is also slow. **Do not** change any network equipment. | Work order marked "voice quality"; include times and pattern — this is usually a network issue, and the pattern is the diagnostic |
| **Voicemail problems** | 1. Confirm you are dialing the correct voicemail access method ⬜ [INSERT]. 2. **If** you cannot remember your voicemail PIN, **then** submit a work order — identity verification is required, per SOP-02 standards. Do not share a PIN with a colleague. 3. **If** the mailbox is full, delete old messages — but preserve any message that documents a business, incident, or regulatory matter (SOP-21). | Work order |
| **Display blank or frozen, buttons unresponsive** | 1. Safe restart (I.2). 2. Confirm the site has power. | Work order |
| **Headset not working** | 1. Confirm the headset is plugged into the headset port, not the handset port. 2. Press the headset button on the phone. 3. Test with the handset to confirm the phone itself works. 4. Check the headset's own power/charge if wireless. | Work order; note the headset model |
| **Phone rings in the wrong place / calls go to the wrong person** | Do not attempt to fix. This is a call-routing configuration issue. | Work order, category IT – Phone/VoIP, note the expected vs actual routing |

### I.4 — Call forwarding, voicemail greetings, and emergency information
1. **Set** a voicemail greeting that states your name, title, organization, and an alternative contact. Sample:
   > "You have reached [Name], [Title] at [Organization Name]. I am not available. Please leave your name, number, and a brief message and I will return your call. For immediate assistance, please contact [Name] at [number]. If this is an emergency, please hang up and dial 911."
2. **Set** an extended-absence greeting when out more than one business day, naming the covering person.
3. **Do not** state in a greeting that you are out of town or away for an extended period.
4. **Request** call forwarding through [Work Order Submission Link] with supervisor approval. **Do not** forward a site's main line or an on-call line to a personal cell phone without written approval from [VP of Operations] and [Director of Information Technology] — forwarding an emergency-reachable number to an unmonitored personal device is a life-safety risk.
5. **Post** at every phone: the site address (for use when calling 911 from a cell phone), the site's main number, the on-call number, [Emergency Contact Number], [IT Support Phone], and the designated outage backup calling method. ⬜ **[Create this posted card and place it at every phone before adoption — Appendix L includes the layout.]**
6. **Verify** quarterly that the posted information is current.

### I.5 — Changes requiring IT action and approval

| Change | Requested by | Approved by | Executed by |
|---|---|---|---|
| New extension / new phone | Supervisor via work order | Department director | IT |
| Extension reassignment (staff change) | Supervisor | Department director | IT (coordinate with SOP-09) |
| Adding/removing a member of a call group | Supervisor | Department director | IT |
| Changing call-group ring order or overflow | Supervisor | [Director of Information Technology] | IT |
| Auto-attendant menu or main-number routing change | Department director | [Chief Operating Officer] | IT + vendor |
| Call forwarding to an external number | Supervisor | Director + [Director of IT] | IT |
| Forwarding an on-call or site main line to a cell | [VP of Operations] | [VP of Operations] + [Director of IT] | IT |
| Voicemail PIN reset | User | Per SOP-02 verification | IT |
| Voicemail box access for a departed employee | Supervisor + HR | [Human Resources Contact] + [Director of IT] | IT |
| Physically moving a phone to another room or site | Supervisor via work order | Director of IT | IT (**E911 address must be updated**) |
| Recording of calls (if any) | — | [Legal Counsel Contact] + [Chief Operating Officer] | ⬜ **[LEGAL REVIEW REQUIRED — New Jersey and federal call-recording consent requirements apply]** |

**Staff may never:** change phone system settings through a vendor portal; contact the VoIP vendor directly; relocate a phone between sites; or share voicemail PINs.

### I.6 — Reporting a phone outage
1. **Determine** scope: one phone, several phones, the whole site, or multiple sites.
2. **If** the whole site is down, **then** **call [IT Support Phone]** (or [Emergency IT Contact] after hours) **immediately** — do not wait on a ticket.
3. **Announce** the backup calling method to all staff on shift and confirm every staff member has a working cell phone or knows the designated backup device.
4. **Notify** the on-call manager and [VP of Operations] if a residential site is affected.
5. **Submit** a work order with: site, number of phones affected, start time, whether internet is also down, backup method in use, and the site's staffing on shift.
6. **Log** the outage start and end times — this feeds the downtime-by-site KPI.
7. **If** the outage exceeds ⬜ [recommend 1 hour] at a residential site, **then** escalate to [Chief Operating Officer].
8. **Verify** restoration by placing a test call in **and** out, and to voicemail, before declaring service restored.

### I.7 — Staff quick-reference

```
PHONE PROBLEM?
│
├─ Is this the ONLY working phone at a residential site?
│     └─ YES → CALL [IT Support Phone] NOW. Announce backup calling method.
│              Confirm 911 can still be reached from a cell. Notify on-call manager.
│
├─ Screen dark?      → Check network cable + power at both ends → Safe restart
├─ No dial tone?     → Try speakerphone → Check handset cord → Safe restart
├─ No incoming?      → Check Do Not Disturb / forwarding icon → Test from a cell
├─ No outgoing?      → Note exact error → Test internal / local / long distance
├─ Bad audio?        → Test handset vs speaker vs headset → Note times → Work order
├─ Voicemail issue?  → Check mailbox full → Work order for PIN (no sharing)
│
├─ SAFE RESTART: unplug network/power cable → wait 30 sec → replug → wait 3 min
│
├─ NEVER: change network settings · swap the two ports on the phone ·
│         move a phone to another site · call the phone vendor directly
│
└─ Still broken → [Work Order Submission Link] (include site, extension, exact error,
                  what you tried, whether internet is also down)
```

## J. Escalation Criteria
| Trigger | Escalate to | Timeframe |
|---|---|---|
| Any site with no working phone | [IT Support Phone] / [Emergency IT Contact] + on-call manager | Immediately |
| Residential site phone outage > 1 hour | [Chief Operating Officer] + [VP of Operations] | At 1 hour |
| Multi-site or organization-wide phone outage | Director of IT → COO → CEO; activate SOP-17 major incident | Immediately |
| 911 call failed or routed to the wrong address | [Director of IT] + vendor + [Chief Operating Officer]; treat as Severity 1 | Immediately |
| Main number / auto-attendant down during business hours | Director of IT + Marketing (external messaging) | 30 minutes |
| Repeated voice-quality problems at one site | Director of IT (likely a network capacity issue — SOP-06) | At third report |

## K. Required Documentation and Record Retention
Work orders (7 years) · outage log with start/end and backup method used (7 years) · configuration change records (7 years) · E911 address registry and verification dates (current + 7 years) · voicemail access approvals (7 years) · quarterly posted-information verification (3 years).

## L. Service-Level Target *(proposed, requiring leadership approval)*
| Situation | First response | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| Site with no working phone (residential) | 15 minutes, 24/7 | 2 hours or verified backup in place |
| Site-wide phone outage (non-residential) | 30 minutes | 4 business hours |
| Single phone down, alternatives available | 4 business hours | 2 business days |
| Voice quality degradation | 1 business day | 5 business days (root cause) |
| Voicemail PIN reset | 4 business hours | Same business day |
| Configuration change (routine) | 1 business day | 3 business days |

## M. Quality-Control Checklist
- ☐ Every site has a documented and tested backup calling method
- ☐ E911 addresses verified for every device, and re-verified after every move
- ☐ Emergency information card posted at every phone, verified quarterly
- ☐ Extension directory current and matched to the HR roster
- ☐ No site main line or on-call line forwarded to an unapproved personal number
- ☐ Departed employees' extensions and voicemail reassigned within SOP-09 timeframes
- ☐ Phone downtime logged by site and reported monthly

## N. Related Policies and Forms
SOP-03 · SOP-06 · SOP-09 · SOP-17 · SOP-25 · Appendix A, L · QR-1

# SOP-06 — INTERNET OUTAGE AND NETWORK DISRUPTION RESPONSE

**B. SOP Number:** ITG-SOP-06 | **Version:** 1.0 | **Effective:** ⬜ [DATE]

## C. Purpose
1. Classify network problems correctly in the first five minutes so the right response is triggered.
2. Keep residential and day program services operating during connectivity loss.
3. Ensure leadership, sites, vendors, and the MSP are notified consistently and once.
4. Capture downtime data by site so that chronic connectivity problems become a budget decision rather than a recurring crisis.

## D. Scope
All internet circuits, wired and wireless networks, VPN and remote access, and cloud-service reachability at all sites and for remote staff.

## E. Owner
**Accountable:** [Chief Operating Officer] · **Responsible:** [Director of Information Technology] · **Support:** [IT Provider Name], ISPs ⬜ [INSERT ISP LIST WITH ACCOUNT NUMBERS AND SUPPORT NUMBERS PER SITE]

## F. Required Systems, Tools, Forms, or Links
[Work Order Submission Link] · [IT Support Phone] · [Emergency IT Contact] · site roster with circuit details (Section 2.3) · ISP support contacts ⬜ [INSERT] · status page ⬜ [INSERT INTERNAL STATUS PAGE OR MASS-NOTIFICATION TOOL] · hotspot inventory ⬜ [INSERT] · downtime forms (SOP-25)

## G. Definitions
| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Single-user issue** | Only one person's device cannot connect. Others at the site are fine. |
| **Single-location issue** | Everyone at one site is affected. |
| **Organization-wide outage** | Multiple sites affected simultaneously. |
| **Wi-Fi issue** | Wireless does not work but wired connections do. |
| **ISP outage** | The internet provider's service is down; internal equipment is fine. |
| **Emergency outage** | Any outage that threatens resident safety, emergency communication, medication documentation, or life-safety systems. |
| **Degradation** | Service is present but slow or unreliable — often harder on operations than a clean outage, and frequently under-reported. |

## H. Roles and Responsibilities
| Role | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| **Employee** | Report promptly; describe scope; switch to documented downtime procedures; do not change any equipment settings |
| **Site supervisor** | Determine scope; confirm emergency communication is available; initiate downtime procedures; notify the on-call manager; log start/end times |
| **IT** | Diagnose, coordinate the MSP and ISP, communicate status, verify restoration, run root-cause review |
| **MSP** | Technical diagnosis and remediation per contract; carrier escalation |
| **VP of Operations** | Program continuity decisions; staffing adjustments |
| **COO** | Declares a major incident; approves emergency spend; communicates to CEO and Board |
| **Finance** | Payroll and billing continuity (SOP-25) |

## I. Procedure

### I.1 — Immediate staff actions (first 5 minutes)
1. **Stop** and check safety first. **If** the outage affects an emergency call system, a fire panel, a nurse call, or door security, **then** notify the supervisor immediately and follow site emergency procedures.
2. **Check** whether your own device alone is affected: ask one colleague at the site to try a website on their device.
3. **Check** whether it is Wi-Fi only: **if** a wired computer works and wireless does not, **then** report it as a Wi-Fi issue.
4. **Restart** only your own device — never the network equipment.
5. **Look** at the internet equipment **without touching it** and note the light pattern (which lights are on, off, solid, blinking, and what color). Photograph it.
6. **Do not** unplug, reset, reboot, or reconfigure routers, switches, firewalls, access points, or modems. **If** a power-cycle is needed, IT or the MSP will direct it step by step. Unauthorized resets frequently turn a 20-minute ISP issue into a multi-day rebuild.
7. **Report** immediately (I.3).
8. **Switch** to the documented downtime procedure for your function (SOP-25).

### I.2 — Determine operational impact (supervisor, no technical changes)
Answer these seven questions and include the answers in the report:
1. Can staff call 911 and the on-call nurse/manager right now? (Phones, cell coverage.)
2. Is medication administration or clinical documentation affected? What is the paper fallback and is it available on site?
3. Is resident transportation affected?
4. Is any life-safety or security system affected (fire panel, alarm, cameras, door access, emergency call)?
5. How many staff and how many individuals served are affected?
6. Is a hard deadline at risk today (payroll cutoff, billing submission, survey response, grant deadline)?
7. Does cellular service work at this site as an alternative?

### I.3 — Classification and response

| Classification | Definition | First action | Notify | Proposed response |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Single-user** | One device | Restart device; submit work order | IT queue | 4 business hours |
| **Wi-Fi only** | Wireless down, wired works | Use wired connection; submit work order | IT queue | 4 business hours |
| **Single-location (non-residential)** | One office or day program | Call [IT Support Phone]; begin downtime procedures | IT, site lead, VP Ops | 1 hour response |
| **Single-location (residential)** | One group home, apartment, or the ICF | **Call [IT Support Phone] / [Emergency IT Contact] immediately**; confirm emergency communication; begin downtime procedures | IT, on-call manager, VP Ops, COO | 15 minutes response, 24/7 |
| **ISP outage** | Provider confirmed down | IT opens ISP ticket and records the provider's reference number and ETA | IT, site lead, COO if >2 hours | Per carrier SLA ⬜ [INSERT] |
| **Organization-wide** | 2+ sites or all cloud access | Activate SOP-17 major incident | Everyone per I.5 | Immediate, continuous |
| **Emergency outage** | Safety, emergency comms, or clinical documentation at risk | 911 if immediate danger; emergency notification; continuous management presence | CEO, COO, VP Ops, Clinical, Compliance | Immediate, continuous |

### I.4 — Decision tree

```
INTERNET / NETWORK PROBLEM
│
├─ Is anyone in immediate danger, or is a life-safety system affected?
│     └─ YES → Follow site emergency procedures. Call 911 if needed.
│              Call [Emergency IT Contact]. Notify on-call manager. → EMERGENCY OUTAGE
│
├─ Can we still call 911 and the on-call nurse from this site?
│     ├─ NO  → EMERGENCY OUTAGE. Call now. Deploy cell phones/hotspot. Consider
│     │         relocating staff communications. Notify VP of Operations + COO.
│     └─ YES → continue
│
├─ Is only MY device affected? (Ask one colleague to test.)
│     └─ YES → Restart my device → Work order (Normal) → DONE
│
├─ Is it only Wi-Fi? (Wired works?)
│     └─ YES → Use wired → Work order (Normal/High by impact) → DONE
│
├─ Is the WHOLE SITE down?
│     ├─ Residential site → CALL [IT Support Phone] NOW → downtime procedures →
│     │                     notify on-call manager + VP Ops → log start time
│     └─ Office/day program → Call [IT Support Phone] → downtime procedures →
│                             notify site lead → log start time
│
├─ Are OTHER SITES also down?
│     └─ YES → ORGANIZATION-WIDE. IT activates SOP-17. COO declares major incident.
│              Single communication channel opened. Updates every 30 minutes.
│
└─ ALWAYS: photograph equipment lights · change nothing · log start time ·
           switch to paper downtime forms · record what could not be completed
```

### I.5 — Notification
1. **Notify in this order** for a site outage: [IT Support Phone] → site supervisor → on-call manager (residential) → [Director of Information Technology] → [VP of Operations].
2. **Notify in this order** for an organization-wide outage: [Director of Information Technology] → [Chief Operating Officer] → [Executive Director/CEO] → VP of Operations, Clinical, Finance, HR, Compliance → all staff via the mass-notification method → external parties if service delivery is affected.
3. **Use one channel** for status updates so staff are not chasing rumors: ⬜ **[INSERT — recommend a mass text/SMS notification tool, because email is unavailable during many outages. This is a required decision; an outage plan that communicates by email fails exactly when it is needed.]**
4. **Update** every 30 minutes for P1/major incidents, even when the update is "no change."
5. **Notify vendors** whose services are affected (EHR, billing, payroll, telephony, alarm monitoring) so they do not interpret silence as a problem on their side.
6. **Notify** ⬜ **[INSERT — determine with Compliance whether any outage duration triggers a notification to DDD, DOH, or a funder]**.

### I.6 — Business continuity during an outage
| Function | Continuity step | Owner | Prepared in advance |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Emergency communication** | Confirm cell coverage; distribute the site's designated backup phone; post 911 address at each phone | Site supervisor | ⬜ Backup method documented per site |
| **Medication administration / clinical documentation** | Switch to paper downtime forms; document times; enter into the system within ⬜ [INSERT] hours of restoration | Clinical / Nursing | ⬜ **Paper downtime packets pre-printed and stored at every site — verify quarterly** |
| **Resident supervision and care** | Unaffected; continue per program policy | Program lead | — |
| **Transportation** | Use printed route sheets and driver cell phones | Fleet / Program | ⬜ Printed route sheets at each site |
| **Payroll** | Use cellular hotspot or an alternate location; contact payroll vendor for deadline extension if needed | Finance / HR | ⬜ Payroll contingency documented with vendor |
| **Medicaid billing** | Hold and batch; confirm submission deadlines and any extension process | Director of Billing / Finance | ⬜ Deadline calendar documented |
| **Facilities and fleet work orders** | Phone intake to [IT Support Phone]/Facilities; log on paper; enter when restored | Facilities | ⬜ Paper intake form (Appendix A printed) |
| **Incident reporting (UIR)** | Paper UIR forms; submit per program policy timelines regardless of system availability | Program / Compliance | ⬜ Paper UIR forms at every site |
| **External communication** | Main number forwarding plan; website notice if public-facing | Marketing + IT | ⬜ Pre-approved notice text (Appendix M) |
| **Food service, laundry, daily living** | Unaffected unless powered systems fail | Site supervisor | — |

### I.7 — Mobile hotspots and alternate locations
1. **Maintain** a hotspot inventory ⬜ **[INSERT — recommend at minimum one hotspot per residential cluster and one per day program, with a documented monthly cost and an owner]**.
2. **Store** hotspots charged, tested monthly, and with an assigned custodian recorded in the asset inventory (SOP-11).
3. **Deploy** a hotspot when: a site outage exceeds ⬜ [recommend 1 hour]; a deadline-critical function is blocked; or clinical documentation cannot be entered.
4. **Prioritize** hotspot bandwidth for: emergency communication, clinical documentation, then payroll/billing, then general email. Do not use a hotspot for streaming, updates, or large downloads.
5. **Connect** only organization-owned devices to the hotspot.
6. **If** the outage is expected to exceed ⬜ [recommend 4 hours] at an administrative site, **then** authorize alternate work locations: another organizational site, or approved remote work per SOP-12.
7. **Never** ask staff to use a public Wi-Fi network (café, library, hotel) to access organizational systems during an outage without VPN and approval (SOP-12.4).
8. **Record** hotspot deployment and data usage — persistent hotspot dependence at a site is evidence for a circuit upgrade or a redundant connection (SOP-22).

### I.8 — Logging, restoration verification, and root-cause review
1. **Log** for every outage: site, classification, start time (actual, not reported), detection method, end time, duration, cause, vendor ticket numbers, affected functions, continuity steps used, and business impact.
2. **Verify restoration** before declaring service restored — test all of: a website, organizational email, the clinical documentation system, the phone system (in and out), the print/scan path, and any site-specific critical application. **Do not** accept the ISP's word that service is restored without an on-site or remote functional test.
3. **Confirm** with the site supervisor that operations are functioning before closing.
4. **Complete** a root-cause review for: any outage over ⬜ [recommend 4 hours]; any residential site outage; any second outage at the same site within 90 days; and any organization-wide outage. Use the SOP-17 post-incident review format.
5. **Assign** corrective actions with owners and dates. Track them to completion in the Technology Governance Committee.
6. **Reconcile** the outage against the ISP's SLA and pursue service credits where available — [Director of Finance] tracks credits received.

### I.9 — Communication templates
Full templates in Appendix M: (1) initial staff notice, (2) site/program team notice, (3) vendor notice, (4) leadership update, (5) restoration notice, (6) extended-outage notice.

## J. Escalation Criteria
| Trigger | Escalate to | Timeframe |
|---|---|---|
| Residential site loses internet | [IT Support Phone] / [Emergency IT Contact] | Immediately |
| Any site cannot reach 911 or on-call | On-call manager + VP Ops + COO; treat as Severity 1 | Immediately |
| Outage > 1 hour at a residential site | [Chief Operating Officer] | At 1 hour |
| Outage > 4 hours at any site | [Chief Operating Officer]; consider major incident | At 4 hours |
| 2+ sites affected | Major incident (SOP-17); COO declares | Immediately |
| Clinical documentation unavailable > ⬜ [INSERT] hours | Chief Clinical Officer + VP Compliance | At threshold |
| Payroll or billing deadline at risk | [Director of Finance] + COO | Immediately |
| Third outage at the same site in 90 days | COO — circuit/vendor decision required | At third occurrence |
| Suspected malicious cause | SOP-08 / SOP-17 immediately; do not treat as a routine outage | Immediately |

## K. Required Documentation and Record Retention
Outage log (7 years) · root-cause reviews (7 years) · ISP tickets and SLA credit records (7 years) · downtime documentation from programs (per clinical/program retention, coordinate with Compliance) · hotspot deployment records (3 years) · restoration verification checklists (3 years).

## L. Service-Level Target *(proposed, requiring leadership approval)*
| Situation | First response | Restoration target |
|---|---|---|
| Residential site fully down | 15 minutes, 24/7 | 4 hours or working alternative (hotspot) within 2 hours |
| Day program / office site down | 1 hour | 8 business hours or alternative |
| Organization-wide | Immediate | Continuous effort; update every 30 minutes |
| Wi-Fi only | 4 business hours | 2 business days |
| Single user | 4 business hours | 1 business day |
| Degraded performance | 1 business day | 5 business days (root cause) |

## M. Quality-Control Checklist
- ☐ Every site's circuit, provider, account number, and support number documented in the roster
- ☐ Backup connectivity option identified and costed for every residential site
- ☐ Paper downtime packets present and current at every site (verified quarterly)
- ☐ Mass-notification channel selected, tested, and known to all supervisors
- ☐ Hotspots inventoried, charged, tested monthly, and assigned
- ☐ Outage log complete with start/end times for every event
- ☐ Root-cause reviews completed for all qualifying outages
- ☐ ISP SLA credits pursued and tracked
- ☐ Semi-annual outage drill completed at a rotating site

## N. Related Policies and Forms
