# SOP-07 — INTERNET AND TECHNOLOGY ACCEPTABLE USE

**B. SOP Number:** ITG-SOP-07 | **Version:** 1.0 | **Effective:** ⬜ [DATE]

## C. Purpose
1. Define what staff may and may not do with organizational technology.
2. Protect the network and data from avoidable exposure.
3. Give supervisors and HR a clear, enforceable standard.
4. Provide the legally required notice regarding monitoring.

⬜ **[HR AND LEGAL REVIEW REQUIRED FOR THIS ENTIRE SOP — particularly I.6 (monitoring notice), which must be mirrored in the Employee Handbook and the annual acknowledgement to be enforceable.]**

## D. Scope
All organizational networks, internet access, devices, systems, software, cloud services, Wi-Fi, mobile devices, remote access, and data — and all personal devices used for organizational work.

## E. Owner
**Accountable:** [Chief Operating Officer] · **Responsible:** [Director of Information Technology] · **Co-owner for enforcement:** [Chief Human Resources Officer] · **Consulted:** Legal, Compliance

## F. Required Systems, Tools, Forms, or Links
Employee Handbook · Appendix I (Exception Request) · Appendix J (Acknowledgement) · [Work Order Submission Link] · approved software register (SOP-13) · [Approved AI Tool List] (SOP-10)

## G. Definitions
| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Acceptable use** | Use that supports the organization's mission and complies with this manual. |
| **Guest Wi-Fi** | A separate wireless network for visitors, with no access to organizational systems. |
| **Personal device (BYOD)** | A device you own, used for organizational work. |
| **Portable storage** | USB drives, external hard drives, SD cards, optical media. |
| **Shadow IT** | Any application or service used for work that IT has not approved or inventoried. |

## H. Roles and Responsibilities
| Role | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| **Employee** | Use technology lawfully and professionally; use only approved software and services; report misuse |
| **Supervisor** | Set expectations; address minor misuse through coaching; refer serious matters to HR |
| **IT** | Provide approved tools; apply technical controls; monitor for security purposes; report findings to HR/COO through the defined path only |
| **HR** | Administer discipline; maintain handbook alignment; retain acknowledgements |
| **Legal** | Approve monitoring language; direct investigations and preservation |

## I. Procedure

### I.1 — Acceptable use
1. **Use** organizational technology primarily for organizational work.
2. **Use** only software, cloud services, and AI tools that appear on the approved registers.
3. **Store** organizational records in [Approved File-Sharing Platform] (Google Shared Drives), not on a local desktop, personal drive, or personal cloud account. Files stored only on a local device are not backed up and are lost when the device fails.
4. **Lock** your screen whenever you step away (a two-second habit that prevents most opportunistic exposure at shared sites).
5. **Keep** devices physically secure (SOP-19).
6. **Install** updates when prompted (SOP-08.12).
7. **Follow** data-classification rules before sharing anything (SOP-14).

### I.2 — Appropriate personal use
1. **Limited personal use** of organizational internet and email is permitted when it: does not interfere with work; does not consume significant bandwidth or storage; incurs no cost; does not violate any policy; and does not occur during direct resident support duties.
2. **Reasonable examples:** checking a personal email account through a browser during a break; reading news; a brief personal phone call; looking up directions.
3. **Not reasonable:** streaming video or music during shift; downloading large personal files; personal cloud backup; gaming; running a personal business; extensive social media during work time.
4. **Personal use carries no expectation of privacy.** See I.6.
5. **At residential and day program sites, personal device use during direct support time is governed by program policy, not this manual** — program supervision requirements take precedence.

### I.3 — Prohibited use
1. Any illegal activity, including unauthorized access to systems or data.
2. Harassment, discrimination, bullying, threats, or creating a hostile environment.
3. Accessing, storing, or transmitting sexually explicit, violent, or hateful material.
4. Downloading or installing unapproved software, browser extensions, or apps.
5. Pirated software, media, or license-key circumvention.
6. Cryptocurrency mining or trading on organizational systems or networks.
7. Visiting sites known to distribute malware, or intentionally bypassing web filtering.
8. Bypassing, disabling, or attempting to defeat any security control — including antivirus, encryption, MFA, web filtering, logging, or device management. **Disabling a security control is never an acceptable response to it being inconvenient; the correct response is an exception request (Appendix I).**
9. Using another person's credentials, or allowing another person to use yours.
10. Accessing records of individuals served, employees, or donors without a job-related need. Curiosity is not a need.
11. Removing organizational data from organizational systems for personal retention.
12. Connecting unauthorized devices to the organizational network (personal routers, switches, access points, network-connected personal equipment, or smart-home devices).
13. Using organizational technology for personal political campaigning, lobbying, or activity that jeopardizes the organization's tax-exempt status.
14. Using organizational branding, email, or systems to represent a personal opinion as the organization's position.
15. Recording audio or video of staff, individuals served, or families without express authorization (⬜ **LEGAL REVIEW — New Jersey consent requirements**).
16. Photographing individuals served on a personal device under any circumstances.
17. Sending organizational data to a personal account, personal cloud storage, or a personal AI tool.

### I.4 — Wi-Fi, personal devices, public networks, and portable storage

**Guest Wi-Fi**
1. **Provide** guest Wi-Fi for visitors, families, and individuals served where appropriate, on a network fully separated from organizational systems.
2. **Do not** connect organization-owned devices to guest Wi-Fi for organizational work.
3. **Change** the guest password ⬜ [recommend quarterly, and immediately after any incident].
4. **Do not** post the guest password where it is visible from outside the building.

**Personal devices (BYOD)**
1. **Personal devices may access organizational email and files only with approval and only with the required protections** (SOP-12): device passcode, encryption, screen lock, current OS, and the ability for the organization to remove organizational data remotely.
2. **Do not** store organizational files locally on a personal device.
3. **Do not** photograph organizational documents, screens, or whiteboards containing confidential information with a personal device.
4. **Understand** that if a personal device is used for organizational work, its organizational data may be subject to legal hold, e-discovery, and remote removal. ⬜ **[HR AND LEGAL REVIEW REQUIRED — BYOD creates real employee-privacy and wage-hour questions; confirm the approach before adoption.]**

**Public Wi-Fi**
1. **Avoid** public Wi-Fi for organizational work.
2. **If** you must use it, **then** connect through the approved VPN ⬜ [INSERT VPN NAME], or use your phone's cellular hotspot instead — cellular is materially safer than open Wi-Fi.
3. **Never** access finance, payroll, HR, or clinical systems over public Wi-Fi without VPN.
4. **Never** ignore a certificate or security warning on a public network.

**Remote work**
1. Follow SOP-12 in full.
2. Secure your work area from household viewing of confidential information; use a privacy screen where needed.
3. Do not allow family members to use an organization-owned device.
4. Use a headset for calls involving confidential information.

**Portable storage**
1. **Default position: USB drives and external storage are not used for organizational data.** Use [Approved File-Sharing Platform].
2. **If** portable storage is operationally necessary, **then** it must be organization-issued, encrypted, inventoried, and approved via Appendix I.
3. **Never** connect a USB drive you found, received unsolicited, or received from a vendor at an event (SOP-08.16).
4. **Never** store the only copy of any record on portable media.

### I.5 — Software approval and license management
1. **Request** all software through Appendix G. See SOP-13 for the full procedure.
2. **Do not** install anything yourself, including free tools, browser extensions, and mobile apps used for work.
3. **Do not** accept a vendor's offer to install software on an organizational device.
4. **Understand** that free is not free: an unapproved free tool that touches resident data can create a HIPAA obligation the organization cannot satisfy because there is no Business Associate Agreement.
5. **IT** maintains the license register, reconciles licenses to active users quarterly, and reports unused-license cost to [Director of Finance] (SOP-13, SOP-22).

### I.6 — Monitoring, audit, and no-expectation-of-privacy notice

⬜ **[LEGAL AND HR REVIEW REQUIRED — this language must be reviewed and mirrored in the Employee Handbook and Appendix J.]**

1. All organizational systems, devices, accounts, and networks are the property of [Organization Name].
2. **Employees should have no expectation of privacy in any communication, file, or activity created, stored, sent, or received on organizational systems or on personal devices used for organizational work**, subject to applicable law.
3. The organization may access, monitor, review, retain, and disclose such information for legitimate business purposes, including: security monitoring, incident investigation, system administration, legal hold and e-discovery, audit and regulatory response, and investigation of suspected policy violations.
4. Routine monitoring is **automated and security-focused** (malware detection, phishing filtering, anomalous sign-in detection, data-loss prevention alerts, license and asset reporting). It is not general reading of employee email.
5. **Targeted review of an individual's account or files requires prior written approval from two of: [Chief Operating Officer], [Chief Human Resources Officer], [Legal Counsel Contact].** This dual-approval requirement exists to protect employees from arbitrary review and to protect the organization from claims of improper access. IT does not conduct targeted review on its own initiative or on a supervisor's verbal request.
6. All targeted reviews are logged: requester, approvers, scope, date range, purpose, and outcome.
7. Monitoring is conducted consistently and without regard to protected characteristics.
8. This notice does not waive attorney-client privilege, and does not apply to employees' protected concerted activity or other legally protected communications. ⬜ **[LEGAL REVIEW — NLRA and New Jersey considerations.]**

### I.7 — Reporting suspected misuse
1. **Report** suspected misuse to your supervisor, [Human Resources Contact], or [IT Support Email].
2. **Report** anonymously through ⬜ [INSERT COMPLIANCE HOTLINE] if you prefer.
3. **If** the suspected misuse involves resident or employee data, **then** also report to [VP Compliance] the same day.
4. **If** the suspected misuse involves a security threat, **then** call [IT Support Phone] immediately — do not wait.
5. **Do not** investigate on your own, access another person's account to gather evidence, or confront the individual.
6. **Retaliation against a good-faith reporter is prohibited** and is itself a disciplinary matter.

## J. Escalation Criteria
| Trigger | Escalate to | Timeframe |
|---|---|---|
| Suspected illegal activity | [Chief Operating Officer] + [Legal Counsel Contact] + CEO | Immediately |
| Harassment or threatening content | [Chief Human Resources Officer] + Legal | Immediately |
| Unauthorized access to resident or employee records | [VP Compliance] + HR + IT | 1 hour |
| Security control bypassed or disabled | [Director of Information Technology] + COO | Immediately |
| Unauthorized device found on the network | IT (isolate) + COO | Immediately |
| Shadow IT discovered holding organizational data | IT + COO; assess BAA and breach exposure with Compliance | 1 business day |
| Request for targeted monitoring | Dual approval per I.6.5 | Before any access |

## K. Required Documentation and Record Retention
Signed acknowledgements (7 years after separation) · monitoring approval and review logs (7 years) · misuse reports and dispositions (7 years, HR) · exception requests (life of exception + 7 years) · unauthorized device findings (3 years).

## L. Service-Level Target *(proposed)*
| Activity | Target |
|---|---|
| Acknowledge a misuse report | 1 business day |
| Isolate an unauthorized device | 4 business hours |
| Complete an approved targeted review | 10 business days |
| Quarterly shadow-IT discovery scan and report | Within 10 business days of quarter end |

## M. Quality-Control Checklist
- ☐ Monitoring notice appears identically in this manual, the handbook, and the acknowledgement
- ☐ 100% of active staff have a current signed acknowledgement
- ☐ Guest Wi-Fi is verified isolated from organizational systems (tested ⬜ annually)
- ☐ Dual-approval log exists for every targeted review; zero unapproved reviews
- ☐ Quarterly shadow-IT discovery completed and reported
- ☐ Approved software register reconciled to installed software
- ☐ No unauthorized network devices found in the last quarterly scan, or all remediated

# SOP-08 — CYBERSECURITY GUIDELINES AND INCIDENT REPORTING

**B. SOP Number:** ITG-SOP-08 | **Version:** 1.0 | **Effective:** ⬜ [DATE]

## C. Purpose
1. Give every employee a small number of rules they can actually remember and apply.
2. Detect and contain security incidents quickly, because containment speed is the single largest determinant of cost.
3. Prevent financial loss from payment fraud, which is the most likely way this organization loses money to an attacker.
4. Meet HIPAA Security Rule, New Jersey breach-notification, funder, and cyber-insurance obligations.

> ## 🛑 IF SOMETHING IS WRONG: **STOP · DISCONNECT · REPORT**
>
> **1. STOP.** Stop typing. Do not click anything else. Do not close windows. Do not delete anything. Do not try to fix it yourself.
> **2. DISCONNECT.** Unplug the network cable and turn off Wi-Fi. **Do NOT power the computer off** unless there is fire, smoke, or a safety hazard — powering off destroys evidence that helps determine whether data was actually taken.
> **3. REPORT.** Call **[IT Support Phone]** immediately. After hours, call **[Emergency IT Contact]**. Then tell your supervisor.
>
> **You will not be disciplined for reporting a mistake promptly and honestly. Not reporting is the serious offense.**

## D. Scope
All users, devices, accounts, systems, networks, data, vendors, and physical records of [Organization Name].

## E. Owner
**Accountable:** [Chief Operating Officer] · **Responsible:** [Director of Information Technology] · **Co-owners:** [VP Compliance] (breach determination and regulatory notification), [Director of Finance] (payment fraud, cyber insurance), [Chief Human Resources Officer] (employee data, discipline) · **Support:** [IT Provider Name]

## F. Required Systems, Tools, Forms, or Links
[IT Support Phone] · [Emergency IT Contact] · [Work Order Submission Link] · phishing report address ⬜ [INSERT] · Appendix E (Incident Report) · Appendix H (Banking Change Verification) · Appendix N (Incident Communication Templates) · cyber insurance policy and carrier hotline ⬜ **[INSERT — carriers frequently require notification within 72 hours and require use of their panel counsel and forensics firm; failing to call first can void coverage]** · [Legal Counsel Contact] · breach counsel ⬜ [INSERT]

## G. Definitions
| Term | Plain meaning |
|---|---|
| **Phishing** | A fake message designed to trick you. |
| **Spear phishing** | Phishing aimed at you personally, using real details about your job. |
| **Business Email Compromise (BEC)** | An attacker uses a real or lookalike email account to redirect money or data. |
| **Malware** | Malicious software. |
| **Ransomware** | Malware that encrypts files and demands payment. |
| **Social engineering** | Manipulating a person into breaking a rule. |
| **Incident** | Any event that may have compromised the confidentiality, integrity, or availability of data or systems. |
| **Breach** | An incident where protected information was actually acquired or disclosed without authorization. **Only [VP Compliance], with Legal, determines whether an incident is a breach.** |
| **Indicator of compromise** | A sign an account or device may be under attacker control. |

## H. Roles and Responsibilities
| Role | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| **Employee** | Recognize and report; never bypass controls; never share credentials; verify money and data requests |
| **Supervisor** | Ensure reporting happens immediately; support staff who report; never pressure staff to skip verification |
| **IT / MSP** | Detect, triage, contain, eradicate, recover; preserve evidence; maintain the incident log |
| **Director of IT** | Incident commander for technical response; assigns severity; escalates |
| **COO** | Declares major incidents; authorizes emergency spend; owns executive communications |
| **VP Compliance** | Conducts HIPAA breach risk assessment; determines and executes regulatory notification |
| **Director of Finance** | Payment-fraud controls; cyber carrier notification; funds recovery attempts |
| **CHRO** | Employee-data incidents; employee notification; disciplinary process |
| **Legal Counsel** | Privilege, breach counsel, law enforcement, contractual notification |
| **CEO** | Board and external stakeholder communication |

## I. Procedure

### I.1 — The ten rules every employee must know

> 1. **Slow down when a message creates urgency.** Urgency is the attacker's primary tool.
> 2. **Verify money and data requests by phone**, using a number you already have — never a number in the message.
> 3. **Never share your password.** Not with IT, not with your boss, not with anyone.
> 4. **Use MFA and never approve a prompt you did not trigger.**
> 5. **Hover before you click.** If the destination does not match the sender, do not click.
> 6. **Do not open unexpected attachments** — call the sender first.
> 7. **Lock your screen** every time you walk away.
> 8. **Install updates** when prompted.
> 9. **Report immediately.** One hour maximum. Sooner is better.
> 10. **If you are not sure — ask.** A two-minute call to [IT Support Phone] is free. An incident is not.

### I.2 — Threat-by-threat guidance

**I.2.1 Phishing.** A message pretending to be someone you trust. Warning signs: urgency, a request to log in, a payment or data request, a mismatched sender address, an unexpected attachment, a generic greeting, poor grammar, a link that does not match the text, an external banner on a message claiming to be internal. **Action:** do not click; report per I.3.

**I.2.2 Business email compromise.** The most financially dangerous threat to this organization. An attacker takes over or imitates a real mailbox — a vendor's, an executive's, or a colleague's — and requests a payment, a banking change, or a data file. The message may be a genuine reply within a real thread. **Action:** any request touching money or personal data gets out-of-band voice verification (I.6). No exceptions, regardless of who appears to be asking.

**I.2.3 Malware.** Software that damages or spies. Signs: sudden slowness, unexpected pop-ups, programs launching on their own, antivirus alerts, disabled security software, new toolbars or extensions, the fan running constantly. **Action:** Stop, Disconnect, Report (I.4).

**I.2.4 Ransomware.** Files become inaccessible, filenames change or gain a strange extension, a ransom note appears, network drives suddenly show errors. **Action:** disconnect immediately — every minute connected spreads the encryption further across shared drives. Then I.4.

**I.2.5 Suspicious links and attachments.** Hover to see the true destination. Watch for lookalike domains (rn instead of m, a hyphen added, .co instead of .com). Treat any attachment you did not expect as hostile until the sender confirms by phone.

**I.2.6 Fraudulent invoice and payment-change requests.** See I.6 — the mandatory verification procedure.

**I.2.7 Executive impersonation.** A message that appears to be from the CEO or COO, often from an outside address or a lookalike domain, often claiming to be in a meeting and unable to talk, asking for gift cards, a wire, a payroll change, or W-2 information. **No executive of [Organization Name] will ever request gift cards, a wire transfer, or employee data by email or text.** Any such message is fraudulent. Report it and tell the named executive by phone.

**I.2.8 Password security.** Long unique passphrases, a password manager, never reused, never shared, never written down at the desk. See SOP-02.

**I.2.9 Multi-factor authentication.** Required on every account that can reach email, PHI, finance, payroll, or HR data. **If you receive an MFA prompt you did not trigger, deny it and report immediately** — it means someone has your password. Repeated prompts ("MFA fatigue") are an active attack in progress.

**I.2.10 Device security.** Lock your screen. Do not leave devices in a vehicle, visible or otherwise. Do not let anyone else use an organizational device. Keep devices encrypted (IT verifies). Do not use an organizational device on a network you do not trust without VPN.

**I.2.11 Software updates.** Install them when prompted; do not defer indefinitely. **If** an update would interrupt direct resident care, **then** defer and complete it at end of shift — and tell IT if you must defer repeatedly, because the correct fix is a maintenance window, not an unpatched device.

**I.2.12 Secure file sharing.** Share from [Approved File-Sharing Platform] with named recipients and the minimum permission. Never "anyone with the link" for confidential data. Review and revoke external shares (SOP-14).

**I.2.13 Remote work and public Wi-Fi.** See SOP-07 I.4 and SOP-12. Prefer cellular hotspot over public Wi-Fi.

**I.2.14 Lost or stolen devices.** Report **immediately**, day or night, to [IT Support Phone] / [Emergency IT Contact]. Also report to your supervisor, and file a police report for theft. IT will remotely lock or wipe. **If** the device held PHI, **then** Compliance is notified the same day. Speed matters: an encrypted device reported quickly is usually not a breach; the same device reported three days later is a much harder analysis.

**I.2.15 USB and removable media.** Never plug in a drive you did not obtain from IT. A "found" or "gifted" USB drive is a common attack. Turn unknown media over to IT.

**I.2.16 Social engineering.** Someone calls claiming to be from IT, a vendor, a state agency, a funder, a family member, or a contractor, and asks you to install something, give access, confirm information, or hold a door. **Verify independently.** Hang up and call the organization back on a known number. **If** someone you do not recognize is in a restricted area or asks you to let them in, **then** politely direct them to the front desk and notify your supervisor (SOP-19).

**I.2.17 Data breaches and privacy incidents.** Any actual or suspected exposure of resident, employee, donor, or financial information — including misdirected email, a lost document, an overheard conversation in a public place, an unlocked screen viewed by a visitor, or a misconfigured share. Report within **1 hour**. Compliance determines breach status; the reporter never makes that call.

**I.2.18 Secure disposal.** Paper containing confidential information goes only into locked shredding containers or a cross-cut shredder — never a trash or recycling bin, never a bag left by a back door. Electronic media is disposed of only through IT with a certificate of destruction (SOP-11.7). Never sell, donate, or discard an organizational device yourself.

### I.3 — Suspected phishing: staff procedure
1. **Do not** click, reply, open attachments, or enter any information.
2. **Do not** forward it to coworkers.
3. **Report** it using Gmail's **Report phishing** option (three-dot menu → Report phishing) **and** forward to ⬜ [PHISHING REPORT ADDRESS].
4. **Tell** your supervisor if it references your specific role, a payment, or a resident.
5. **If** it impersonates an executive or requests money or data, **then** also notify [Director of Finance] and call [IT Support Phone].
6. **Delete** the message only after IT confirms — and not before.
7. **If you clicked, replied, entered your password, or approved an MFA prompt:**
   a. **Call [IT Support Phone] immediately** — do not email.
   b. Disconnect from the network.
   c. Do not attempt to change your own password first; IT will coordinate reset and session revocation together, because changing the password alone does not evict an attacker who already holds a session token.
   d. Tell your supervisor.
   e. Write down exactly what you did and when.
   f. **Expect support, not blame.**

### I.4 — Suspected malware or ransomware: staff procedure
1. **Stop** using the device immediately.
2. **Disconnect** from the network: unplug the network cable and turn off Wi-Fi.
3. **Do not** power off or restart the device unless there is a physical safety hazard.
4. **Do not** run antivirus scans, delete files, or attempt cleanup.
5. **Do not** connect any USB drive or phone to the device.
6. **Photograph** the screen, including any ransom note, with a phone.
7. **Call [IT Support Phone]** immediately. After hours, **[Emergency IT Contact]**.
8. **Notify** your supervisor and your site lead.
9. **Warn** colleagues at the site verbally not to use shared drives until IT advises.
10. **If** files on a shared drive are affected, **then** tell IT immediately — this indicates spread beyond one device and changes the response completely.
11. **Stay available**; IT will need to know what you were doing immediately before.
12. **If** the device is needed for resident care, **then** tell IT so a replacement is prioritized; do not keep using the suspect device.

### I.5 — IT/MSP response sequence (summary — full detail in SOP-17)
1. **Triage and assign severity** (I.7) within 30 minutes of report.
2. **Preserve evidence** before remediation: capture logs, images, and screenshots.
3. **Contain:** isolate devices, disable accounts, revoke sessions and tokens, block indicators, disable forwarding rules.
4. **Notify** per I.8.
5. **Assess scope:** what accounts, what devices, what data, what time window.
6. **Eradicate:** remove malware, close the entry vector, reset affected credentials.
7. **Recover:** restore from verified clean backups (SOP-15), validate integrity before returning to service.
8. **Document** continuously in the incident record.
9. **Conduct** a post-incident review within 10 business days (I.10).
10. **Never** pay a ransom, negotiate, or communicate with an attacker without [Executive Director/CEO], [Legal Counsel Contact], and the cyber carrier — payment decisions carry legal, insurance, and OFAC sanctions implications.

### I.6 — Finance verification procedure: payments, banking changes, wires, and ACH

> ### 💰 THE RULE THAT PREVENTS THE MOST EXPENSIVE LOSS
> **No change to how, where, or to whom money is sent is ever made based on an email, a letter, a fax, a text, or a phone call the organization received. Verification is always outbound, by voice, to a number already in our vendor file, with a person whose name we already have.**

**Applies to:** vendor banking or remittance changes · new vendor setup · wire transfers · ACH changes · employee direct deposit changes · payroll data requests · gift card requests · unusual, duplicate, or urgent invoices · changes to a payment address · requests to expedite or bypass normal approval · any request to change contact information on a vendor record.

**Procedure:**
1. **Stop.** Do not process, do not reply, do not forward the request to accounting for action.
2. **Complete** the Vendor Banking Change Verification Form (Appendix H).
3. **Look up** the vendor's contact information **in the existing vendor master file** — never in the request, its signature block, its letterhead, or an attached document.
4. **Call** a known contact at that number. **If** the only number you have is the one in the request, **then** find an independent number (the vendor's published main line, the executed contract, a prior invoice predating the request) or escalate to [Director of Finance].
5. **Speak with a person.** Voicemail is not verification. Email confirmation is not verification. A reply on the same thread is not verification.
6. **Confirm** the specific change: the exact new account and routing numbers, the effective date, and who authorized it on the vendor's side.
7. **Document** on Appendix H: who you called, at what number, from what source, whom you spoke with, what they confirmed, date and time, and your name.
8. **Obtain** a second approval: [Director of Finance] for changes below ⬜ [INSERT THRESHOLD]; [Director of Finance] **and** [Chief Operating Officer] above it; add [Executive Director/CEO] above ⬜ [INSERT SECOND THRESHOLD].
9. **Apply** a hold period before the first payment on new banking details ⬜ [recommend 1 business day] and send a confirmation notice to the vendor's **previously known** contact — so that if the request was fraudulent, the real vendor tells you.
10. **Process** only after all of the above.
11. **Escalate** immediately, before processing, if any of these appear: pressure to act today; a request to keep it confidential; a claim that the CEO or COO has already approved it; a slight difference in the sender's domain; a reply-to address different from the sender; a new contact person you have never dealt with; a request that arrives just before a holiday, a weekend, or a known executive absence.

**Gift cards:** [Organization Name] does not purchase gift cards in response to an email, text, or chat request from any executive, under any circumstance. Any such request is fraudulent. Report it and call the named executive directly.

**Payroll and W-2 data:** requests for employee tax, payroll, or direct-deposit data are verified in person or by voice with the named requester before any data leaves the system. Direct-deposit change requests from employees are verified with the employee by voice or in person by HR/Payroll.

**If funds have already been sent:**
1. **Call the bank immediately** and request a recall — the first 24 hours are decisive, and the first 4 are best.
2. **Notify** [Director of Finance] and [Chief Operating Officer] immediately.
3. **Notify** the cyber insurance carrier ⬜ [INSERT CARRIER HOTLINE] — before any remediation, because carriers impose conditions.
4. **Report** to the FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3.gov) and local law enforcement.
5. **Preserve** all emails and records; do not delete anything.
6. **Notify** [Legal Counsel Contact].
7. **Treat** the event as a potential account compromise and run the full I.5 sequence on the affected mailboxes.

### I.7 — Incident severity levels

| Severity | Definition | Examples | Notify | Response |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **SEV 1 — Critical** | Confirmed breach, active ransomware, organization-wide outage, resident safety impact, PHI exposure at scale, funds sent to a fraudster | Ransomware encrypting shared drives; confirmed exfiltration of resident data; complete loss of clinical systems | CEO, COO, Compliance, Finance, Legal, cyber carrier, Board Chair | Immediate, continuous, 24/7; major incident declared (SOP-17) |
| **SEV 2 — High** | Confirmed account compromise, malware on a device with sensitive access, misdirected PHI to an external party, attempted BEC that reached processing, single-site critical outage | Executive mailbox compromised; a laptop with PHI stolen; an invoice fraud caught at the bank | COO, Director of IT, Compliance, Finance if financial | Within 1 hour; continuous until contained |
| **SEV 3 — Moderate** | Contained event with limited exposure | Phishing click with no credential entry; malware blocked by endpoint protection; a misdirected internal email with limited PII | Director of IT; Compliance if any PHI | Within 4 business hours |
| **SEV 4 — Low** | No exposure; informational | Reported phishing not clicked; blocked login attempt; policy question | Director of IT queue | Within 1 business day |

**Severity is assigned by [Director of Information Technology] and may be raised by anyone. It is never lowered without the Director of IT's documented agreement. When in doubt, start higher.**

### I.8 — Notification and escalation responsibilities

| Who | Notified for | By whom | When |
|---|---|---|---|
| [Director of Information Technology] | All incidents | Reporter / MSP | Immediately |
| [Chief Operating Officer] | SEV 1–2 | Director of IT | Within 30 minutes |
| [Executive Director/CEO] | SEV 1; SEV 2 involving PHI, funds, or reputation | COO | Within 1 hour |
| [VP Compliance] | Any incident involving PHI, resident, or employee data | Director of IT | Within 1 hour |
| [Director of Finance] | Any financial incident; any compromise of a payment-capable account | Director of IT | Immediately |
| [Chief Human Resources Officer] | Employee data incidents; incidents involving employee conduct | COO | Within 1 hour |
| [Legal Counsel Contact] | SEV 1; any suspected breach; any law-enforcement contact | COO / CEO | Within 4 hours |
| Cyber insurance carrier | Per policy ⬜ [INSERT — commonly required before engaging any vendor] | Director of Finance | Per policy, typically ≤72 hours |
| [Board Chair] / [Board Treasurer] | SEV 1; material financial loss | CEO | Within 24 hours |
| Regulators (HHS OCR, NJ Division of Consumer Affairs, DDD, DOH, CMS) | Per breach determination | VP Compliance + Legal | Per statute ⬜ **[LEGAL REVIEW]** |
| Affected individuals | Per breach determination | VP Compliance + Legal | Per statute (HIPAA: without unreasonable delay, ≤60 days) |
| Funders / grantors | Per contract terms ⬜ [INSERT] | COO + Development | Per contract |
| MSP | All incidents | Director of IT | Immediately |

**Do not** communicate externally about an incident — to media, families, funders, or on social media — without [Executive Director/CEO] and [Legal Counsel Contact] approval. Use Appendix N templates only after approval.

### I.9 — Incident documentation requirements
Every incident record (Appendix E) must contain:
1. Incident number, date and time detected, date and time occurred (if known), date and time reported.
2. Reporter name, role, site, and how it was reported.
3. Description in plain language.
4. Systems, accounts, devices, and data types involved.
5. Number of individuals potentially affected.
6. Whether PHI, PII, financial, donor, or employee data was involved.
7. Severity assigned, by whom, and when — including any change in severity and why.
8. Containment actions with timestamps.
9. Evidence preserved and where it is stored.
10. Root cause.
11. Notifications made, to whom, by whom, when.
12. Compliance breach determination and its basis.
13. Financial impact: direct loss, recovery, response cost, insurance claim.
14. Corrective actions with owners and due dates.
15. Closure approval by [Director of Information Technology] and, for SEV 1–2, [Chief Operating Officer].

### I.10 — Post-incident review
1. **Hold** the review within 10 business days of closure for SEV 1–2, and quarterly in aggregate for SEV 3–4.
2. **Attend:** Director of IT, COO, MSP, and the affected function's leader; add Compliance, Finance, HR, Legal as relevant.
3. **Answer** six questions:
   a. What happened, on a minute-by-minute timeline?
   b. How was it detected, and how long did detection take?
   c. What worked in the response?
   d. What failed or was slower than it should have been?
   e. What control would have prevented it, and what would that control cost?
   f. What changes are we making, who owns each, and by when?
4. **Focus on systems, not individuals.** A review that produces "the employee should have been more careful" as its root cause has not found the root cause.
5. **Assign** corrective actions and track to completion at the Technology Governance Committee.
6. **Update** training content, this manual, and the phishing simulation program based on findings.
7. **Report** the summary to the CEO, and to the Board for SEV 1.

### I.11 — Annual cybersecurity training outline (⬜ ~45–60 minutes)

| Module | Minutes | Content |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Why this matters here | 5 | What we hold: resident PHI, employee data, donor records, $48M in operations. What an incident costs in dollars, service disruption, and trust. Real nonprofit and IDD-sector examples. |
| 2. Phishing and BEC | 12 | Live examples from our own inbox (anonymized). Hover practice. The external banner. How to report. What happens after you report. |
| 3. Passwords and MFA | 8 | Passphrases. The password manager. Never share. MFA prompts you did not trigger. Lost MFA device. |
| 4. Money and data requests | 8 | The verification rule. Gift card fraud. Vendor banking changes. Payroll diversion. Role-play a call. |
| 5. Devices and physical security | 6 | Lock your screen. Lost devices. USB drives. Tailgating. Printer output. Shredding. |
| 6. Working remotely and on the road | 5 | Public Wi-Fi. Hotspots. Home office confidentiality. Vehicle security. |
| 7. AI safety | 6 | What may and may not be entered into an AI tool. The approved list. See SOP-10. |
| 8. Reporting and the no-blame rule | 5 | Stop-Disconnect-Report. The 1-hour standard. Why we do not discipline honest reporting. |
| 9. Knowledge check | 5 | 10 questions; ⬜ [recommend 80%] to pass; retake permitted. |

**Role-specific add-ons:** Finance/AP (+15 min payment fraud deep dive) · Executives and assistants (+10 min impersonation and travel risk) · Supervisors (+10 min how to support a reporting employee) · IT and MSP (+ incident response tabletop).

**Delivery:** ⬜ [INSERT PLATFORM] · assign within 30 days of hire and annually · track completion by department · report completion in the executive dashboard.

### I.12 — Fifteen realistic red-flag examples

| # | Scenario | Red flags | Correct action |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | An email from "[CEO Name]" sent from a Gmail address: *"I'm going into a board meeting and can't take calls. I need you to purchase four $500 gift cards for a staff recognition today. Send me the codes and I'll reimburse you."* | External address; urgency; unavailable by phone; gift cards; secrecy | Do not reply. Report. Call the CEO directly on a known number. |
| 2 | A message from a long-standing maintenance vendor: *"We've changed banks. Please update our ACH details for this month's invoice."* On a real reply thread. | Banking change by email; even a genuine thread can be a compromised mailbox | Execute I.6. Call the vendor at the number in the vendor master file. |
| 3 | *"Your Google Workspace password expires in 24 hours. Click here to keep your account active."* Link goes to a lookalike domain. | Threat + deadline; credential harvesting; domain mismatch | Do not click. Report phishing. IT never sends password links like this. |
| 4 | A caller says he is from our IT provider and needs remote access to fix an "urgent server problem," and asks the front desk to install a tool. | Unsolicited; urgency; requests installation; bypasses the ticket process | Hang up. Call [IT Support Phone]. Real MSP work has a ticket. |
| 5 | An email claiming to be from DDD or the Department of Health: *"Attached is a survey citation requiring response within 48 hours."* Attachment is a macro-enabled document. | Regulator fear + attachment; agencies rarely deliver citations this way | Do not open. Report. Verify through [VP Compliance] and known agency contacts. |
| 6 | A grant "award notification" from an unfamiliar foundation asking for a wire of a processing fee or for bank details to deposit funds. | Unsolicited award; fee to receive money; bank details requested | Report to [Director of Development] and Finance. Verify the foundation independently. |
| 7 | A text to a program director: *"This is [COO Name]. I need your cell number for a quick task, I'm in meetings all day."* | New number; executive; urgency; moving to text where verification is harder | Do not respond. Call the COO's known number. Report. |
| 8 | A property management company emails a "new remittance address" for a lease payment, on new letterhead. | Payment redirection; letterhead is not verification | Execute I.6. Call the property manager at the number on the executed lease. |
| 9 | An invoice arrives for landscaping at a site — same vendor name, slightly different amount, slightly different logo, a new remit-to address. | Duplicate/altered invoice; small changes designed to pass a glance | Do not pay. Match to a purchase order and prior invoices. Call the vendor. |
| 10 | A staff member gets repeated MFA push prompts at 2:00 AM and taps approve to stop them. | MFA fatigue attack — the attacker already has the password | Deny, then call [IT Support Phone] immediately. If approved: report at once; this is an active compromise. |
| 11 | An email from "Payroll" asking every employee to "re-verify direct deposit information" through a linked form. | Mass payroll diversion attempt | Do not click. Report. HR/Payroll never collects bank details through an emailed link. |
| 12 | A USB drive in a padded envelope arrives addressed to the Director of Finance, labeled "Q3 Financial Analysis — Confidential." | Unsolicited media; targeted at a high-value role | Do not connect it to anything. Give it to IT. Report. |
| 13 | A vendor technician arrives on site for HVAC work with no appointment and asks a DSP to let him into the utility closet and the office. | No appointment; requests access to restricted space; uses a helpful employee | Do not admit. Verify with Facilities and the supervisor. Escort only if verified (SOP-19). |
| 14 | A shared Google Drive link arrives from a colleague's real address, titled "Updated Resident Roster — please review," but the link asks you to sign in again. | Compromised colleague account; re-authentication prompt is credential harvesting | Do not sign in. Call the colleague. Report. |
| 15 | A donor "wants to make a $50,000 gift" but needs the organization's bank account details and a signed form emailed back today. | Urgency; large gift; account details by email; pressure | Route to [Director of Development] and [Director of Finance]. Never send account details by email; use the approved process. |

## J. Escalation Criteria
Any SEV 1 or SEV 2 escalates immediately per I.8. In addition: any employee may escalate directly to [Chief Operating Officer] if they believe an incident is not being taken seriously; any incident involving PHI escalates to [VP Compliance] within 1 hour regardless of severity; any financial incident escalates to [Director of Finance] immediately; any suspected insider misconduct escalates to CHRO and Legal, not to the individual's supervisor.

## K. Required Documentation and Record Retention
Incident records (7 years; HIPAA-related 6 years from resolution or determination) · phishing reports and dispositions (7 years) · breach risk assessments (6 years, Compliance) · notification records (6 years) · post-incident reviews and corrective actions (7 years) · training completion (7 years after separation) · phishing simulation results (3 years) · cyber insurance claims (permanent).

## L. Service-Level Target *(proposed, requiring leadership approval)*
| Activity | Target |
|---|---|
| Staff report a suspected incident | Within 1 hour of noticing |
| IT/MSP acknowledge a reported incident | 30 minutes, 24/7 |
| Severity assigned | 30 minutes |
| SEV 1 containment initiated | Immediately; contained or isolated within 1 hour |
| SEV 2 containment | Within 4 hours |
| Compromised account disabled | 15 minutes from confirmation |
| Compliance notified of a PHI incident | 1 hour |
| Cyber carrier notified | Per policy ⬜ [INSERT] |
| Post-incident review completed | 10 business days (SEV 1–2) |
| Corrective actions closed | 60 days, or a documented extension approved by COO |

## M. Quality-Control Checklist
- ☐ Stop-Disconnect-Report card posted at every site and every shared workstation
- ☐ MFA enforced on 100% of accounts reaching email, PHI, finance, payroll, or HR
- ☐ Phishing report path tested and monitored, with a named backup monitor
- ☐ Payment verification form (Appendix H) used on 100% of banking changes — audited quarterly by Finance
- ☐ Cyber insurance notification requirements documented and known to Finance and IT
- ☐ Incident log complete, with severity, timestamps, and closure approval
- ☐ Annual training completion ≥ ⬜ [recommend 95%]
- ☐ Phishing simulation run on schedule; repeat-clicker follow-up completed
- ☐ Tabletop exercise completed in the last 12 months
- ☐ Post-incident corrective actions tracked to closure with none overdue >60 days

# SOP-09 — USER CREATION, ACCESS CHANGES, AND USER REMOVAL

**B. SOP Number:** ITG-SOP-09 | **Version:** 1.0 | **Effective:** ⬜ [DATE]

## C. Purpose
1. Ensure new staff can work on day one without anyone sharing credentials to make that happen.
2. Ensure access matches the job a person holds **today**, not the accumulation of every job they have held.
3. Remove access immediately on separation, which is the single highest-value control in this manual and the one most often missed in high-turnover environments.
4. Produce a defensible access record for HIPAA, funder, and audit review.

> **Why this SOP matters disproportionately here.** Direct support is a high-turnover role across the IDD sector. In an organization with roughly 688 employees, even moderate turnover means hundreds of onboarding and offboarding events a year. At that volume, offboarding cannot depend on anyone remembering — it has to be a triggered mechanism with a measured completion rate. That measurement is the KPI in Part IV.

## D. Scope
All accounts, access rights, and credentials for all systems, for all user types, at all sites.

## E. Owner
**Accountable:** [Chief Operating Officer] · **Responsible:** [Director of Information Technology] · **Trigger owner:** [Chief Human Resources Officer] (employee events) · **Approvers:** hiring manager and department director · **Consulted:** Clinical (clinical roles), Finance (financial roles), Compliance

> **Authority note.** The trigger for nearly every action in this SOP originates in HR, which is a peer function. This SOP cannot be implemented by IT alone. Section 6.2 applies: obtain HR co-ownership of the trigger, the timing standard, and the completion measurement before publication.

## F. Required Systems, Tools, Forms, or Links
Appendix B (New User Access Request) · Appendix C (User Change Request) · Appendix D (Offboarding) · Appendix F (Asset Assignment) · [Work Order Submission Link] · HR system of record ⬜ [INSERT] · access matrix ⬜ [INSERT LINK — must be built before adoption]

## G. Definitions
| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Provisioning** | Creating accounts and granting access. |
| **Deprovisioning** | Disabling and removing access. |
| **Role-based access** | Access assigned by job role rather than person-by-person. |
| **Least privilege** | The minimum access needed to do the job. |
| **Separation of duties** | No one person controls an entire high-risk transaction end to end. |
| **Orphaned account** | An active account with no current, verified owner. |
| **Access matrix** | The table mapping each role to the systems and permission levels it receives. |

## H. Roles and Responsibilities
See the RACI matrix in I.15.

## I. Procedure

### I.1 — Access principles
1. **Grant by role, not by request.** Build the access matrix once; assign a role thereafter. Person-by-person provisioning does not scale to 688 employees and produces inconsistent access no one can audit.
2. **Grant the minimum.** "The same access as [colleague]" is not an acceptable request — it propagates one person's accumulated over-provisioning to everyone who follows.
3. **Time-box** non-employee access: every contractor, intern, volunteer, temporary worker, and vendor account carries a mandatory expiration date not exceeding ⬜ [recommend 90 days], renewable with approval.
4. **Separate duties** in financial systems: the person who sets up a vendor cannot be the person who approves payment to that vendor.
5. **Privileged access is separate.** Administrators use a standard account for daily work and a distinct privileged account only for administrative tasks.
6. **Every account has one identifiable human owner.** Service accounts and shared program accounts have a named accountable owner recorded in the access register.
7. **No access without an approved request.** Verbal requests, hallway requests, and "just add them quickly" requests are declined, including from executives.

### I.2 — Access matrix (to be completed before adoption)

*Permission levels to use: **N** = none · **R** = read only · **W** = create/edit · **A** = approve · **X** = administrative.*

### I.3 — New employee onboarding
1. **Hiring manager submits** Appendix B at least ⬜ **[recommend 5 business days]** before the start date. Include: legal name, preferred name, job title, department, site(s), supervisor, start date, employment type, role (from the access matrix), any access beyond the role with justification, equipment needs, phone/extension needs, and whether PHI access is required.
2. **Department director approves** the request, and specifically approves any access beyond the standard role profile.
3. **HR confirms** the hire is complete: offer accepted, background check cleared per policy, and required pre-access training assigned.
4. **If** the role requires PHI access, **then** confirm HIPAA training is assigned and scheduled before access is enabled.
5. **IT provisions** per the access matrix: account, mailbox, group memberships, Shared Drive access, application accounts, MFA enrollment prompt, device assignment (Appendix F), phone extension, printer access, badge request to Facilities.
6. **IT records** everything in the access register with the approval reference.
7. **IT delivers** credentials securely: temporary credential per SOP-02 delivered to the supervisor in person or by verified voice contact — never by email to a personal address, and never to an address supplied in the request.
8. **Employee completes** MFA enrollment, first-login password change, and IT orientation before independent system use.
9. **Employee signs** Appendix J (acknowledgement) and Appendix F (asset assignment).
10. **Supervisor confirms** on day one that access works, and submits a work order for any gaps the same day.
11. **IT verifies** at 30 days that no excess access was granted and that the account is in active use. **If** an account has never been used at 30 days, **then** confirm with the supervisor and disable if the person did not start.

### I.4 — Non-employee access

| Type | Sponsor | Additional requirements | Max duration before renewal |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Contractor / consultant** | Contracting department director | Executed contract; confidentiality agreement; BAA if PHI is involved; named individuals only, no shared accounts | 90 days ⬜ |
| **Temporary / agency staff** | Hiring supervisor | Agency agreement; background screening per policy; access limited to the assignment | Assignment end date, max 90 days ⬜ |
| **Intern / student** | Supervising manager | School agreement; confidentiality agreement; no PHI access without Clinical approval and training | Program end date |
| **Volunteer** | Volunteer coordinator | Confidentiality agreement; access only if operationally necessary | 90 days ⬜ |
| **Vendor with system access** | [Director of Information Technology] | Contract; BAA if PHI; named individuals; access logged and reviewed monthly; MFA required; remote access only through the approved method | 90 days ⬜ |
| **Board member** | [Executive Director/CEO] | Board portal and organizational email only; **no access to resident records, PHI, HR files, or operational systems**; confidentiality agreement | Term of service, reviewed annually |
| **Auditor / surveyor** | [VP Compliance] | Time-boxed, read-only where possible, escorted, activity logged | Engagement duration |

1. **Every** non-employee account is visibly marked as such ⬜ [recommend a naming convention or directory attribute, e.g. display name suffix "(Contractor)"] so that staff can tell from an email who is and is not an employee.
2. **Every** non-employee account has an expiration date set at creation. **If** the expiration is reached without a renewal approval, **then** the account disables automatically.
3. **Sponsors** are responsible for notifying IT the day the engagement ends — the same standard as an employee separation.

### I.5 — New-user setup checklist

> **☐ NEW USER SETUP — IT CHECKLIST**
> ☐ Appendix B received with director approval
> ☐ HR confirmation of completed hire received
> ☐ Role identified in the access matrix; exceptions justified and approved
> ☐ Google Workspace account created; display name and title correct
> ☐ Mailbox and signature (Appendix K) configured
> ☐ Group and distribution list memberships applied
> ☐ Shared Drive access applied per role
> ☐ MFA enrollment required at first sign-in
> ☐ Application accounts created (list each, with permission level)
> ☐ Clinical/EHR access — Clinical approval on file if applicable
> ☐ Financial system access — separation of duties verified if applicable
> ☐ VPN / remote access if required
> ☐ Phone extension, voicemail box, call group membership
> ☐ Printer / scan-to-email access
> ☐ Badge / physical access requested from Facilities
> ☐ Device assigned, encrypted, enrolled in management, recorded on Appendix F
> ☐ Approved AI tool access if applicable, with SOP-10 training assigned
> ☐ Training assigned: IT orientation, security awareness, HIPAA (if applicable), AI (if applicable)
> ☐ Acknowledgement (Appendix J) signed and filed
> ☐ Access register updated with all of the above
> ☐ Supervisor confirmation of working access on day one
> ☐ 30-day post-provisioning verification completed

### I.6 — Access changes: transfers, promotions, role changes, and leave
1. **Submit** Appendix C as soon as the change is known — before the effective date.
2. **State explicitly** what access is being **added** and what is being **removed**. A change request that only adds is the mechanism by which over-provisioning accumulates; IT will return a request that does not address removal.
3. **Approvals:** the losing supervisor confirms what should be removed; the gaining supervisor requests what is needed; the department director approves.
4. **Apply** removals on the effective date, not "eventually." **If** a transition period is genuinely needed, **then** state the end date on the form; the maximum overlap is ⬜ [recommend 14 days] and it expires automatically.
5. **Leave of absence:**
   a. **If** the leave exceeds ⬜ [recommend 30 days], **then** suspend access on the leave start date (account disabled, data retained, mailbox preserved).
   b. **If** the leave is shorter, **then** maintain access but arrange delegation for coverage rather than credential sharing.
   c. **If** the leave is for a reason involving a workplace conduct concern, **then** HR directs the access posture and IT acts on HR's written instruction only.
   d. **Reinstate** access on documented return-to-work confirmation from HR, with a password reset per SOP-02 and confirmation that the role has not changed.
6. **Changed responsibilities without a title change** still require Appendix C. This is the most commonly skipped path and the most common source of audit findings.
7. **Promotion into a supervisory role** triggers supervisor training on this SOP within 30 days.

### I.7 — Offboarding: standard
1. **HR or the supervisor notifies IT** using Appendix D **as soon as the separation date is known** — not on the last day.
2. **Confirm** the exact last working day and last time of system access. These are often different; the form captures both.
3. **Disable** all access at the designated time, per I.9.
4. **Collect** property per I.10.
5. **Handle** the mailbox and files per I.11.
6. **Complete** the removal checklist (I.12) and file it.
7. **Reconcile** within 5 business days: confirm zero active sessions, zero remaining application accounts, zero remaining physical access.

### I.8 — Offboarding: urgent and involuntary
1. **HR, or [Chief Human Resources Officer] in HR's absence, notifies [Director of Information Technology] directly by phone** — not by email, and not through a ticket — for any involuntary separation, any separation involving a conduct concern, or any situation where a person may act against the organization's interests.
2. **IT disables all access simultaneously and immediately**, ideally at the moment the separation conversation begins — coordinate the exact time with HR in advance.
3. **Actions, executed together:** disable the account; revoke all active sessions and tokens; revoke mobile device access and wipe organizational data from personal devices; disable VPN and remote access; disable badge and physical access; disable all application accounts; remove from all distribution lists and call groups; change any shared credentials the person knew; revoke MFA enrollments; disable any API keys or service accounts they controlled.
4. **Preserve** the mailbox, files, and device — do not delete or re-image anything. **If** litigation or investigation is anticipated, **then** [Legal Counsel Contact] issues a legal hold before any change (SOP-21).
5. **Review** the person's recent activity where warranted, with the dual approval required by SOP-07 I.6.5.
6. **If** the person held privileged access, **then** rotate the credentials of every administrative account and shared credential they could have known, and review administrative logs for the prior ⬜ [recommend 90 days].
7. **If** the person had payment authority, **then** notify [Director of Finance] immediately and review recent payment and vendor-master activity.
8. **Notify** the MSP to remove the person from any vendor-side authorization list — an often-missed step that leaves a former employee able to authorize changes by phone.
9. **Document** everything with timestamps.

### I.9 — Required timing for disabling access

| Separation type | Access disabled | Approval | Escalation if missed |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Involuntary / for cause** | **Immediately — at or before the separation meeting** | CHRO by phone to Director of IT | COO notified within 1 hour |
| **Resignation with notice** | **By end of the last working day** ⬜ *(recommend: within 1 hour of shift end)* | HR via Appendix D | COO notified at 24 hours |
| **Contractor / vendor end of engagement** | **By the engagement end date** | Sponsor | Director of IT at 24 hours |
| **Temporary / agency assignment end** | **By the assignment end date** | Supervisor | Director of IT at 24 hours |
| **Job abandonment / no call no show** | Per HR determination, **not later than 3 business days** ⬜ | CHRO | COO at day 3 |
| **Death of an employee** | Within 1 business day, handled with care; mailbox preserved; personal effects and personal data handled by HR with the family | CHRO | — |
| **Leave > 30 days** | On leave start date (suspend, not delete) | HR | — |
| **Board member term end** | Within 5 business days | CEO's office | — |

**Measurement:** IT reports offboarding completion within the required timeframe as a monthly KPI (Part IV). ⬜ **[Proposed target: 100% for involuntary, ≥98% overall. This target requires HR agreement, because IT cannot meet it without timely notice.]**

### I.10 — Recovering organizational property
1. **Supervisor collects** and records on Appendix D: laptop, desktop, tablet, mobile phone, hotspot, headset, docking station, monitors, chargers, and any peripherals; keys (building, room, vehicle, cabinet, medication, mailbox); badges and access cards; fuel cards and credit cards; vehicle keys and fobs; paper records and files; printed resident information; parking passes; uniforms/identification; and any personally purchased equipment used for work that contains organizational data.
2. **Verify** each item against the asset inventory (SOP-11) and Appendix F assignment records.
3. **If** property is not returned, **then**: document specifically what is outstanding; HR sends a written request; **if** still not returned within ⬜ [INSERT] days, **then** escalate to [Chief Operating Officer] and [Legal Counsel Contact], and remotely wipe the device.
4. **If** a device cannot be recovered, **then** treat it as a lost device under SOP-08.2.14, including the PHI assessment.
5. **Remove** organizational data from any personal device used for work, and document the removal and the employee's acknowledgment.
6. **Recover** credentials: confirm the person no longer holds shared credentials, vendor portal logins, social media access, domain registrar access, bank portal access, or any account where they were a named contact. ⬜ **[BUILD REQUIRED: a register of every external account where an employee is a named authorized contact. This is the most commonly missed offboarding element and the one with the longest tail of risk.]**

### I.11 — Mailbox, files, forwarding, and preservation
1. **Convert** the mailbox to a preserved state rather than deleting it. **Do not delete a mailbox for at least** ⬜ **[recommend 12 months]**, and never while a hold is in effect.
2. **Set an autoresponder** on the departed employee's address for ⬜ [recommend 90 days]:
   > "Thank you for your message. [Name] is no longer with [Organization Name]. Please direct [subject area] inquiries to [Name, Title] at [email] or [phone]. Your message has been forwarded to the appropriate team member."
3. **Delegate** the mailbox to the supervisor or a designated colleague for business continuity — with written HR approval, a defined duration, and a documented scope. Delegation is logged.
4. **Do not** auto-forward a departed employee's mail to a personal address under any circumstance.
5. **Transfer** file ownership: move files from the individual's Drive to the appropriate Shared Drive before the account is suspended. ⬜ **[If staff currently store organizational records in personal My Drive rather than Shared Drives, fix that as a standing practice — otherwise every departure becomes a records-recovery project.]**
6. **Reassign** the phone extension and voicemail; record a new greeting; do not leave a departed employee's voice on the organization's phone system.
7. **Remove** from the website, directory, email signatures, distribution lists, and any external-facing listing.
8. **Preserve** everything unchanged if a legal hold is in effect; the hold supersedes every step above.

### I.12 — User removal checklist

> **☐ OFFBOARDING — IT CHECKLIST** *(attach to Appendix D)*
> ☐ Appendix D received, with last working day and last access time
> ☐ Separation type confirmed with HR (voluntary / involuntary / other)
> ☐ Legal hold status checked with Legal/Compliance **before** any change
> ☐ Google Workspace account suspended; **all sessions and tokens revoked**
> ☐ MFA enrollments revoked
> ☐ Mobile device access revoked; organizational data wiped from personal devices
> ☐ VPN / remote access disabled
> ☐ All application accounts disabled — list each with date/time: ⬜
> ☐ Clinical/EHR access removed (Clinical notified)
> ☐ Financial / payroll / HR system access removed (Finance and HR notified)
> ☐ Removed from distribution lists, Shared Drives, call groups, on-call rotations
> ☐ Removed from vendor portals and MSP authorization lists
> ☐ Shared credentials the person knew have been changed
> ☐ Privileged credentials rotated (if applicable) and admin logs reviewed
> ☐ Badge / physical access disabled; keys collected and logged
> ☐ Devices collected, verified against inventory, and wiped or reissued
> ☐ Files transferred to Shared Drive; ownership reassigned
> ☐ Mailbox preserved; autoresponder set; delegation approved and time-boxed
> ☐ Phone extension reassigned; voicemail greeting replaced
> ☐ Website, directory, and public listings updated
> ☐ Asset inventory updated (SOP-11)
> ☐ Access register updated and closed
> ☐ Checklist signed by [Director of Information Technology] and supervisor
> ☐ Reconciliation completed within 5 business days

### I.13 — Quarterly access review
1. **Generate** a report of every user and their access, by system, from the access register and each system's own report.
2. **Distribute** to each supervisor a list of their team members' access.
3. **Supervisor certifies** in writing, within ⬜ [recommend 10 business days]: each person still works here; each person still needs each access listed; any access to be removed is identified.
4. **IT removes** flagged access within 5 business days and records it.
5. **IT identifies** orphaned accounts — active accounts with no current owner, no supervisor certification, or no sign-in in ⬜ [recommend 60 days] — and disables them after confirmation.
6. **Escalate** non-responding supervisors to their director after the deadline; escalate to [Chief Operating Officer] after 20 business days.
7. **Report** completion percentage in the executive dashboard. **A review that is 70% complete is not a completed review** — report the true number.
8. **Retain** signed certifications as audit evidence.

### I.14 — Privileged account review
1. **Review quarterly**, separately and more rigorously than the general review.
2. **List** every privileged account: domain/Google super admin, application administrators, financial system administrators, VoIP administrator, MSP accounts, security tool administrators, service accounts, break-glass accounts, and vendor administrative access.
3. **For each, confirm:** a named human owner; continued business need; MFA enforced; the account is separate from the owner's daily-use account; last-used date; and that the account's activity log has been reviewed.
4. **Remove** any privileged access no longer required, immediately.
5. **Approve** the reviewed list jointly: [Director of Information Technology] and [Chief Operating Officer] sign.
6. **Report** to the Technology Governance Committee: total privileged accounts, change from prior quarter, and any exceptions.
7. **Target:** ⬜ *(proposed)* the number of privileged accounts should be small, known, and flat or declining. An increasing count without a stated reason is a finding.

### I.15 — RACI responsibility matrix

**R** = Responsible (does the work) · **A** = Accountable (owns the outcome) · **C** = Consulted · **I** = Informed

| Activity | Employee | Hiring Mgr / Supervisor | Dept Director | HR | IT | MSP | System Owner | Finance | Compliance | COO |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Submit new user request (Appendix B) | — | **R** | **A** | C | I | — | C | — | — | I |
| Approve access beyond standard role | — | R | **A** | C | C | — | **R** | C | C | I |
| Confirm hire complete / screening cleared | — | I | I | **R/A** | I | — | — | — | — | — |
| Provision accounts and access | — | I | I | I | **R/A** | R | C | — | — | — |
| Assign and record device | **R** (sign) | R | I | I | **A** | R | — | — | — | — |
| Deliver credentials securely | I | C | — | — | **R/A** | R | — | — | — | — |
| Complete required training | **R** | **A** | I | **R** | C | — | — | — | C | I |
| Sign acknowledgement | **R** | I | — | **A** | I | — | — | — | — | — |
| Submit access change (Appendix C) | — | **R** | **A** | C | I | — | C | — | — | — |
| Identify access to REMOVE on change | — | **R** | **A** | C | C | — | C | — | — | — |
| Notify IT of separation (standard) | — | **R** | I | **A** | I | — | — | — | — | I |
| Notify IT of separation (urgent/for cause) | — | C | I | **R/A** (by phone) | I | — | — | I | I | **I** |
| Disable access within required timeframe | — | I | I | C | **R/A** | R | C | I | I | I |
| Check legal hold before changes | — | — | — | C | **R** | — | — | — | **A** | I |
| Collect physical property | **R** | **R/A** | I | C | C | — | — | — | — | I |
| Rotate privileged credentials after departure | — | — | — | I | **R/A** | R | C | — | — | I |
| Mailbox preservation and delegation | — | C | I | **A** | **R** | R | — | — | C | I |
| File transfer to Shared Drive | C | **R** | I | — | **A** | R | — | — | — | — |
| Quarterly access certification | — | **R** | **A** | C | **R** (report) | C | C | C | C | **A** (completion) |
| Privileged account review | — | — | — | — | **R** | C | C | — | C | **A** |
| Report offboarding KPI | — | — | I | C | **R** | — | — | — | I | **A** |
| Audit / survey evidence production | — | — | — | C | **R** | C | C | C | **A** | I |

## J. Escalation Criteria
| Trigger | Escalate to | Timeframe |
|---|---|---|
| Involuntary separation | CHRO → Director of IT by phone | Immediately, before the meeting |
| Access not disabled within required timeframe | Director of IT → COO | Same day |
| Request to provision without approval | Director of IT; decline and log | Immediately |
| Orphaned or unexplained active account found | Director of IT → COO | 1 business day |
| Departed employee still holds external account access | Director of IT + Finance + COO | Immediately |
| Supervisor fails quarterly certification | Director → COO | After 20 business days |
| Privileged account count increases without justification | COO + Technology Governance Committee | At quarterly review |
| Former employee attempts access | Director of IT + CHRO + Legal; treat as a security incident | Immediately |

## K. Required Documentation and Record Retention
Access requests, changes, and removals with approvals (7 years after separation) · access register (current + 7 years) · quarterly certifications (7 years) · privileged reviews (7 years) · asset assignment and return records (7 years) · mailbox delegation approvals (7 years) · property recovery documentation (7 years).

## L. Service-Level Target *(proposed, requiring leadership approval)*
| Activity | Target |
|---|---|
| New user request submitted before start date | ≥5 business days |
| Account provisioned after complete approved request | 3 business days, and always before start date |
| Access change applied | 2 business days from effective date |
| Standard offboarding disable | By end of last working day |
| Urgent offboarding disable | Immediately, ≤15 minutes from HR notice |
| Property recovery completed | 5 business days |
| Offboarding reconciliation | 5 business days |
| Quarterly access review completed | 30 days from quarter end |

## M. Quality-Control Checklist
- ☐ Access matrix built, approved by each system owner, and current
- ☐ 100% of accounts traceable to an approved request
- ☐ Zero accounts provisioned on verbal request
- ☐ Every non-employee account has an expiration date
- ☐ Offboarding completion within timeframe ≥ target; involuntary at 100%
- ☐ Zero orphaned accounts at quarterly review, or all documented and remediated
- ☐ Privileged account list reviewed and jointly signed each quarter
- ☐ External-contact register maintained and checked at every departure
- ☐ No organizational records held solely in individual Drives (sampled quarterly)
