# PART IV — EXECUTIVE GOVERNANCE

## 4.1 How to Read the Control Register

For each major policy area, the register states ten things in a fixed order. If any line is blank when this manual is adopted, that area is not yet under management.

| Line | Question it answers |
|---|---|
| **1. Risk** | What operational risk are we managing? |
| **2. Minimum control** | What is the least we must do? |
| **3. Accountable executive** | Who answers to the CEO and Board for this? |
| **4. Responsible staff** | Who does the work? |
| **5. Approval authority** | Who can approve, waive, or except? |
| **6. Documentation** | What evidence must exist? |
| **7. KPI** | What number does leadership review? |
| **8. Review frequency** | How often? |
| **9. Escalation threshold** | At what number does this reach an executive? |
| **10. Failure impact** | What happens if the control fails — financial, legal, operational, resident-service, reputational, cybersecurity? |

**Abbreviations:** COO = [Chief Operating Officer] · CEO = [Executive Director/CEO] · DIT = [Director of Information Technology] · DF = [Director of Finance] · CHRO = [Chief Human Resources Officer] · VPC = [VP Compliance] · CCO = [Chief Clinical Officer] · VPO = [VP of Operations] · DFF = [Director of Facilities & Fleet Management] · MSP = [IT Provider Name]

---

## 4.2 Control Register

### CR-01 — Email Usage and Management (SOP-01)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Disclosure of PHI, personnel, donor, or financial data through misdirected, forwarded, or compromised email; loss of business records |
| **Minimum control** | Organizational accounts only; MFA; external-sender banner; no auto-forwarding to external domains; Vault retention; secure method for external PHI; mandatory phishing reporting |
| **Accountable executive** | COO |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; MSP; every employee |
| **Approval authority** | COO (policy); DIT (technical config); CHRO+Legal (monitoring language); VPC (PHI transmission method) |
| **Documentation** | Vault retention config; forwarding-rule alerts; phishing reports and dispositions; misdirected-email incident reports; delegation approvals |
| **KPI** | Phishing reports/month; median report time; confirmed email-related privacy incidents; % accounts with MFA; forwarding-rule alerts |
| **Review frequency** | Monthly (dashboard); quarterly (distribution lists, delegation) |
| **Escalation threshold** | Any PHI misdirection → VPC within 1 hour; any mailbox compromise → COO immediately; >2 email privacy incidents in a quarter → COO review |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** breach response, notification, and credit monitoring costs; potential OCR penalty. **Legal:** HIPAA and NJ breach notification obligations; litigation. **Operational:** mailbox lockdowns disrupt scheduling and billing. **Resident service:** disclosure of a person's service relationship. **Reputational:** local press; family and funder confidence. **Cyber:** a compromised mailbox is the standard launchpad for payment fraud. |

### CR-02 — Password, Lockout, and MFA Support (SOP-02)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Account takeover via social engineering of the reset process; credential sharing defeating all access control and audit |
| **Minimum control** | No reset without live verified identity; never on email alone; tiered verification; temporary credentials out-of-band and single-use; MFA mandatory; no MFA bypass |
| **Accountable executive** | COO |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; MSP helpdesk |
| **Approval authority** | DIT (Tier 3); DIT + second executive (Tier 4); COO (any time-boxed alternative method) |
| **Documentation** | Reset ticket with verification data points, approver, delivery channel, anomaly check; MFA revocation/re-enrollment records; break-glass log |
| **KPI** | % resets with documented verification (target 100%); resets/month by tier; repeat lockouts; social-engineering attempts reported; % accounts with MFA |
| **Review frequency** | Monthly reset-log review; quarterly privileged review |
| **Escalation threshold** | Any email-only reset (target zero) → COO; any pressure to bypass → DIT same day; 3+ lockouts for one user in 7 days → root-cause investigation |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** a single takeover of a payment-capable account can exceed six figures. **Legal:** HIPAA access-control failure. **Operational:** mass lockout halts documentation and billing. **Resident service:** clinical documentation access loss. **Reputational:** "they gave away the password." **Cyber:** account takeover is the entry point for most serious incidents. |

### CR-03 — Work Order and Support Request Process (SOP-03)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Unreported problems, invisible recurring failures, no data for capital planning, staff working around broken technology |
| **Minimum control** | Single intake link; mandatory site and asset fields; priority matrix; acknowledgement and closure confirmation; recurring-issue flag at 3 tickets/90 days |
| **Accountable executive** | COO |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT (IT queue); DFF (Facilities/Fleet queue); MSP; all supervisors |
| **Approval authority** | COO (SLA targets); queue owners (priority changes); COO (recurring-issue spend above threshold) |
| **Documentation** | Ticket record with root cause, cost, and closure confirmation; recurring-issue reviews; monthly queue reports |
| **KPI** | Open tickets by priority and age; first-response and resolution attainment; repeat incidents by site/device/vendor/type; satisfaction; % tickets with a valid site |
| **Review frequency** | Weekly (aging); monthly (dashboard) |
| **Escalation threshold** | P1 unacknowledged 15 min; P1 open 4 hours → COO; any ticket at 2× target; 3+ tickets on one asset in 90 days |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** emergency replacement instead of planned; unrecovered vendor SLA credits. **Legal:** inability to evidence maintenance in a survey or claim. **Operational:** chronic downtime normalized. **Resident service:** delayed repairs in people's homes. **Reputational:** staff stop reporting. **Cyber:** unreported anomalies never reach IT. |

### CR-04 — Printing and Confidential Output (SOP-04)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Confidential documents left in trays or printed to wrong sites; injury from unauthorized repair; uncontrolled print cost; data on retired MFD storage |
| **Minimum control** | Secure/PIN release on shared MFDs; immediate pickup; locked shredding at every site; no internal repair by staff; certificate of destruction on disposal |
| **Accountable executive** | COO |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT (devices); DFF (placement, supply logistics); site leads |
| **Approval authority** | DIT (device changes); COO (fleet replacement); VPC (confidential output incidents) |
| **Documentation** | Work orders; device service and cost history; consumable usage; disposal certificates; output incident reports |
| **KPI** | Printer tickets by device; repeat-failure devices; cost per site; % MFDs with secure release; disposal certificates on file |
| **Review frequency** | Quarterly (cost and recurring devices); annual (fleet) |
| **Escalation threshold** | Unretrievable confidential output → VPC 1 hour; 3+ tickets on a device in 90 days; service cost >50% of replacement |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** print waste; emergency replacements. **Legal:** HIPAA exposure from tray output and unwiped devices. **Operational:** missed mailing and payroll deadlines. **Resident service:** none directly. **Reputational:** visible sloppiness with sensitive documents. **Cyber:** MFDs are networked devices and an overlooked attack surface. |

### CR-05 — Telephony (SOP-05)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | A residential site unable to reach 911 or on-call clinical support; emergency responders sent to a wrong E911 address |
| **Minimum control** | Documented and tested backup calling method per site; E911 address verified per device and after every move; no unapproved forwarding of site or on-call lines; posted emergency card at every phone |
| **Accountable executive** | COO |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; VoIP vendor; site supervisors |
| **Approval authority** | DIT (config); VPO + DIT (on-call/site-line forwarding); COO (auto-attendant and main number); Legal (call recording) |
| **Documentation** | Outage log with start/end and backup method used; E911 registry and verification dates; configuration change records |
| **KPI** | Phone downtime minutes by site; sites with a tested backup method (target 100%); E911 verification currency; time to restore |
| **Review frequency** | Monthly (downtime); quarterly (E911 and posted cards) |
| **Escalation threshold** | Any site with no working phone → immediate; residential outage >1 hour → COO; any failed or misrouted 911 call → SEV 1 |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** modest direct cost; severe liability exposure. **Legal:** licensing and negligence exposure if emergency contact fails. **Operational:** site isolation. **Resident service:** delayed emergency response — the highest-consequence failure in this manual. **Reputational:** severe. **Cyber:** toll fraud and voicemail compromise. |

### CR-06 — Internet and Network Availability (SOP-06)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Loss of clinical documentation, billing, communication, and safety systems at one or many sites |
| **Minimum control** | Classification within 5 minutes; single communication channel; paper downtime packets on site; hotspot availability; outage logging; root-cause review |
| **Accountable executive** | COO |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; MSP; ISPs; site supervisors; VPO |
| **Approval authority** | COO (major incident, emergency spend, redundant circuits); DIT (technical) |
| **Documentation** | Outage log; ISP tickets and SLA credits; restoration verification; root-cause reviews; hotspot deployment records |
| **KPI** | Downtime minutes by site; outages per site per quarter; time to restore; % sites with verified downtime packets; SLA credits recovered |
| **Review frequency** | Monthly; quarterly by-site trend |
| **Escalation threshold** | Residential outage >1 hour → COO; any outage >4 hours; 2+ sites → major incident; 3rd outage at a site in 90 days → circuit/vendor decision |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** lost billing days; overtime for paper catch-up. **Legal:** documentation timeliness failures. **Operational:** organization-wide work stoppage. **Resident service:** clinical documentation and medication records on paper. **Reputational:** repeated outages erode staff confidence. **Cyber:** outages mask and enable intrusions. |

### CR-07 — Acceptable Use and Monitoring (SOP-07)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Misuse creating legal liability; shadow IT holding regulated data; unenforceable discipline because notice was never given |
| **Minimum control** | Published policy with signed annual acknowledgement; handbook alignment; dual approval for targeted monitoring; quarterly shadow-IT discovery |
| **Accountable executive** | COO (policy); CHRO (enforcement) |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; supervisors; HR |
| **Approval authority** | Two of COO/CHRO/Legal for targeted review; COO for exceptions |
| **Documentation** | Signed acknowledgements; monitoring approval and review logs; misuse reports and dispositions; shadow-IT findings |
| **KPI** | % staff with current acknowledgement; misuse reports and outcomes; shadow-IT applications found; unauthorized devices found |
| **Review frequency** | Annual (policy and acknowledgement); quarterly (discovery) |
| **Escalation threshold** | Any suspected illegal activity → CEO and Legal immediately; shadow IT holding PHI → VPC 1 business day; any monitoring without dual approval → COO |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** litigation and settlement. **Legal:** wrongful-discipline and privacy claims if notice is deficient. **Operational:** unmanaged tools break at the worst time. **Resident service:** data in uncontracted systems. **Reputational:** employment claims are public. **Cyber:** shadow IT is unpatched and unmonitored by definition. |

### CR-08 — Cybersecurity and Incident Reporting (SOP-08)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Ransomware halting operations; business email compromise diverting funds; breach of resident or employee data |
| **Minimum control** | MFA everywhere; Stop-Disconnect-Report; 1-hour reporting standard; severity model; out-of-band payment verification; immutable backups; cyber insurance conditions met |
| **Accountable executive** | COO (overall); DF (payment fraud and insurance); VPC (breach determination) |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; MSP; every employee |
| **Approval authority** | COO (major incident, emergency spend); CEO + Legal + carrier (ransom or law enforcement); VPC (notification) |
| **Documentation** | Incident records with timeline and severity; breach risk assessments; notification records; post-incident reviews; carrier notifications |
| **KPI** | Incidents by severity; median time to report, contain, and resolve; phishing report rate and click rate; % MFA coverage; overdue corrective actions; attempted payment fraud detected |
| **Review frequency** | Monthly; SEV 1 immediately to CEO and Board Chair |
| **Escalation threshold** | Any SEV 1 → CEO 1 hour, Board Chair 24 hours; any PHI incident → VPC 1 hour; any funds sent → DF and carrier immediately; corrective action overdue >60 days |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** ransomware recovery in a $48M organization routinely reaches seven figures including downtime; BEC losses are often unrecoverable after 24–48 hours. **Legal:** HIPAA penalties, NJ notification, contractual breach, litigation. **Operational:** days to weeks of degraded operations across 24/7 sites. **Resident service:** loss of clinical records and care continuity. **Reputational:** the most damaging category — donor, funder, family, and referral confidence. **Cyber:** compromise typically recurs where root cause is not addressed. |

### CR-09 — Access Lifecycle (SOP-09)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Former staff retaining access; accumulated over-provisioning; orphaned accounts; inability to evidence access control in audit |
| **Minimum control** | Role-based access matrix; approved request before provisioning; immediate disable for involuntary separations; quarterly access and privileged reviews; time-boxed non-employee access |
| **Accountable executive** | COO; CHRO co-owns the trigger |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; supervisors; HR |
| **Approval authority** | Department director (access); DIT + COO (privileged review sign-off); CHRO (urgent offboarding direction) |
| **Documentation** | Requests, changes, removals with approvals; access register; signed quarterly certifications; privileged review sign-off; property recovery records |
| **KPI** | Offboarding completed within required timeframe (%); involuntary at 100%; access review completion (%); orphaned accounts; privileged account count and trend; provisioning lead time |
| **Review frequency** | Monthly (offboarding); quarterly (access and privileged reviews) |
| **Escalation threshold** | Any missed offboarding timeframe → COO same day; any orphaned account; any privileged count increase without justification; supervisor non-certification at 20 business days |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** unused license cost; theft or fraud by a retained account. **Legal:** HIPAA access-control finding; the most commonly cited deficiency in audits of organizations this size. **Operational:** new staff unable to work on day one. **Resident service:** improper access to records. **Reputational:** a former employee incident is difficult to explain. **Cyber:** dormant accounts are a preferred attacker foothold. |

### CR-10 — Artificial Intelligence Governance (SOP-10)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | PHI or personnel data entered into public AI tools; AI-influenced decisions about people's services, housing, or employment; fabricated content in grant, Board, or regulatory documents |
| **Minimum control** | Approved tool list; data-classification rule before use; absolute prohibition on high-risk decisions; human review and verification; BAA for any PHI tool; training before access |
| **Accountable executive** | COO; AI Governance Committee |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; every user; supervisors |
| **Approval authority** | DIT (low risk); COO (confidential); Committee + COO + VPC (PHI or high-risk); CEO + Legal (high-risk use cases) |
| **Documentation** | Tool register with approved data classification; vendor assessments; BAAs; training records; incident reports; committee minutes |
| **KPI** | Approved tools and total cost; users trained (%); AI incidents; unauthorized tools found; documented benefit per tool |
| **Review frequency** | Quarterly (committee); annually (full risk review and tool re-assessment) |
| **Escalation threshold** | Any PHI entered into AI → VPC and COO within 1 hour; any AI-influenced high-risk decision → CEO and Legal immediately; unapproved tool found → COO 1 business day |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** breach costs; wasted subscription spend; grant clawback for inaccurate reporting. **Legal:** HIPAA; employment-decision regulation; IP and defamation exposure; funder misrepresentation. **Operational:** rework and lost credibility of outputs. **Resident service:** a decision made or shaped by a tool with no accountability. **Reputational:** an IDD provider mishandling AI around disabled people's data is a uniquely damaging story. **Cyber:** data exfiltration through an unapproved tool leaves no log. |

### CR-11 — IT Asset Inventory and Disposal (SOP-11)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Unknown, unencrypted, or untracked devices; data leaving on disposed equipment |
| **Minimum control** | One inventory; 100% tagged, encrypted, and enrolled; annual physical verification; certificate of destruction on every disposal |
| **Accountable executive** | COO | 
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; site supervisors; Finance (ledger reconciliation) |
| **Approval authority** | DIT (disposal); COO (bulk replacement); Finance (capitalization) |
| **Documentation** | Inventory record; Appendix F assignments; disposal certificates; annual verification reports |
| **KPI** | % devices inventoried, encrypted, and managed; devices unaccounted at verification; device age distribution; disposals with certificates |
| **Review frequency** | Monthly (compliance %); annual (physical verification and ledger reconciliation) |
| **Escalation threshold** | Any unencrypted device in use; any device unaccounted for; any disposal without a certificate |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** unbudgeted replacement; insurance claim denial without records. **Legal:** breach from a disposed device. **Operational:** no basis for lifecycle planning. **Resident service:** device failures at sites. **Reputational:** "we don't know where our computers are." **Cyber:** unmanaged devices cannot be patched or wiped. |

### CR-12 — Mobile and Remote Work Security (SOP-12)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Loss of a device carrying PHI; access from uncontrolled networks; organizational data stranded on personal devices |
| **Minimum control** | Passcode, encryption, current OS, management enrollment, remote wipe capability; VPN for internal access; no local storage of organizational files |
| **Accountable executive** | COO; CHRO co-owns BYOD terms |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; employees; supervisors |
| **Approval authority** | DIT (technical); CHRO + Legal (BYOD policy); COO (exceptions) |
| **Documentation** | BYOD approvals and acknowledgments; compliance reports; wipe records; remote work agreements |
| **KPI** | % devices compliant; non-compliant devices blocked; lost/stolen devices; median time to wipe |
| **Review frequency** | Monthly |
| **Escalation threshold** | Lost device → immediate; PHI found stored locally → VPC same day; non-compliant device accessing data |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** breach response. **Legal:** HIPAA; employee privacy claims from an improperly scoped wipe. **Operational:** mobile staff unable to work. **Resident service:** community-based staff cut off. **Reputational:** lost-laptop stories travel. **Cyber:** unmanaged mobile is a direct route into the tenant. |

### CR-13 — Software Procurement and Shadow IT (SOP-13)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Unvetted applications holding regulated data; duplicated spend; click-through agreements binding the organization without a BAA |
| **Minimum control** | Appendix G for all software; overlap screening; security and TCO assessment; BAA before PHI; central contracting; quarterly reconciliation and discovery |
| **Accountable executive** | COO; DF co-owns contracting |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; Finance; department directors |
| **Approval authority** | Tiered by data sensitivity and cost (SOP-13 I.4); CEO above threshold |
| **Documentation** | Requests and approvals; contracts and BAAs; software register; reconciliations; shadow-IT findings |
| **KPI** | Applications in register; % with BAA where required; license utilization %; unused-license cost; shadow-IT found per quarter; renewal reviews completed on time |
| **Review frequency** | Quarterly |
| **Escalation threshold** | Shadow IT holding PHI → VPC 1 business day; vendor refuses BAA; renewal inside 30 days without review; utilization below ⬜ [recommend 70%] |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** duplicate and unused subscriptions are the most common recoverable waste at this scale. **Legal:** unenforceable terms; HIPAA without a BAA. **Operational:** unsupported tools fail with no recourse. **Resident service:** data fragmentation. **Reputational:** funder findings on data handling. **Cyber:** unvetted applications with broad OAuth scopes. |

### CR-14 — Data Classification and Secure Sharing (SOP-14)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Over-broad file sharing; records held only in individual drives; PHI transmitted insecurely |
| **Minimum control** | Four-level classification; Shared Drives as default storage; named-recipient sharing; quarterly external-share review; approved secure transmission for PHI |
| **Accountable executive** | COO; VPC co-owns privacy determinations |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; data owners; all staff |
| **Approval authority** | Data owner (classification); VPC (PHI transmission method); COO (exceptions) |
| **Documentation** | Classification decisions; quarterly external-share certifications; exposure incident reports; consent records |
| **KPI** | External shares of Confidential/Restricted content; shares revoked; review completion %; records held outside Shared Drives (sampled); privacy incidents |
| **Review frequency** | Quarterly |
| **Escalation threshold** | Any Restricted data in a public-link share; any exposure → VPC 1 hour; review completion below 100% |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** breach costs. **Legal:** HIPAA minimum-necessary and disclosure findings. **Operational:** records lost at separation. **Resident service:** disclosure of service relationships. **Reputational:** data-handling findings in surveys. **Cyber:** open shares are discoverable and indexable. |

### CR-15 — Backup and Disaster Recovery (SOP-15)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Inability to recover after ransomware, deletion, corruption, or vendor failure |
| **Minimum control** | Defined RPO/RTO per critical system; independent backup of cloud platforms; at least one immutable off-site copy; quarterly restore testing; annual DR exercise |
| **Accountable executive** | COO |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; MSP; system owners |
| **Approval authority** | COO (RPO/RTO and associated cost); DIT (technical) |
| **Documentation** | RPO/RTO approvals; job logs; restore test records; DR exercise reports; insurance condition evidence |
| **KPI** | Backup success rate; restore tests completed and passed; measured recovery time vs RTO; systems meeting RPO; DR exercise completed |
| **Review frequency** | Monthly (jobs); quarterly (restore tests); annual (DR exercise) |
| **Escalation threshold** | Failed restore test → COO immediately; backup failure unresolved 48 hours; any RPO/RTO unachievable with current funding |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** the difference between a bad week and an existential event; this is the control that determines whether ransomware is survivable without payment. **Legal:** inability to produce records under hold or audit. **Operational:** permanent data loss. **Resident service:** loss of clinical history. **Reputational:** catastrophic. **Cyber:** attackers target backups first — immutability is the specific counter. |

### CR-16 — Vendor and MSP Management (SOP-16)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Paying for service levels we do not receive; vendors with unmanaged access to data; unknown exit costs creating lock-in |
| **Minimum control** | Vendor register with tier and BAA status; contracted SLAs reconciled to this manual; quarterly Tier 1 scorecards; documented switching cost; complete vendor offboarding |
| **Accountable executive** | COO; DF co-owns contracting |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; Finance |
| **Approval authority** | COO (Tier 1 renewal/replacement); DF (contracts); VPC (BAA) |
| **Documentation** | Contracts and BAAs; scorecards; escalation records; security assessments; deletion certificates at offboarding |
| **KPI** | MSP first-response and resolution attainment; scorecard totals by vendor; renewals reviewed on time; vendors without BAA (target zero); active vendor accounts past engagement end (target zero) |
| **Review frequency** | Monthly (MSP operations); quarterly (business review and scorecards) |
| **Escalation threshold** | Two consecutive months of missed SLA → COO; vendor security incident → immediate; vendor access active after end date |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** paying full price for degraded service; unrecovered credits; renewal on unfavorable terms. **Legal:** vendor breach without a BAA becomes our liability. **Operational:** slow support compounds every other control. **Resident service:** delayed repairs. **Reputational:** third-party incidents are still our incidents. **Cyber:** vendor access is a leading intrusion vector. |

### CR-17 — Incident Management and Major-Incident Communications (SOP-17)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Disorganized response extending duration and cost; contradictory communications; unreliable after-the-fact reconstruction |
| **Minimum control** | Single Incident Commander and Business Lead; one communication channel; fixed update cadence; contemporaneous timeline; out-of-band communication method; review within 10 business days |
| **Accountable executive** | COO |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; all function leads; Communications |
| **Approval authority** | COO (declaration and stand-down); CEO + Legal (external communication) |
| **Documentation** | Incident record with timeline; communications issued; post-incident review; corrective action tracking |
| **KPI** | Major incidents; time to declare; time to first communication; update cadence adherence; reviews completed on time; corrective actions overdue |
| **Review frequency** | Per incident; monthly aggregate |
| **Escalation threshold** | SEV 1 → CEO 1 hour; incident at 2× expected duration → COO reassesses resourcing; any corrective action overdue >60 days |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** every additional hour of a major incident has a direct labor and revenue cost. **Legal:** poor documentation weakens insurance claims and regulatory defense. **Operational:** duplicated and conflicting effort. **Resident service:** sites improvising without direction. **Reputational:** inconsistent messaging is what stakeholders remember. **Cyber:** slow containment multiplies scope. |

### CR-18 — Change Management (SOP-18)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Self-inflicted outages from untested, unapproved, or uncommunicated changes — including vendor-initiated ones |
| **Minimum control** | Change request with rollback plan; risk-tiered approval; advance communication; maintenance window agreed with Clinical and Operations; complete change log |
| **Accountable executive** | COO |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; MSP; system owners |
| **Approval authority** | DIT (normal); DIT + COO (high risk); COO + CEO (platform migration) |
| **Documentation** | Change requests and approvals; change log; rollback and verification records; failed-change reviews |
| **KPI** | Changes by type; change success rate; changes causing incidents; unlogged changes (target zero); advance-notice compliance |
| **Review frequency** | Monthly |
| **Escalation threshold** | Any change causing an outage → SOP-17; any change outside process; emergency change undocumented after 1 business day |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** avoidable downtime cost. **Legal:** documentation gaps in a regulated system. **Operational:** the most common preventable cause of disruption. **Resident service:** unexpected loss of clinical systems mid-shift. **Reputational:** staff lose confidence in IT. **Cyber:** unreviewed changes silently weaken controls. |

### CR-19 — Physical Security (SOP-19)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Theft of devices and records; unauthorized access to network closets and restricted spaces; keys and badges held by former staff |
| **Minimum control** | Locked network/equipment spaces with logged access; clean-desk standard; locked shredding at every site; key and badge registers reconciled quarterly; visitor and contractor verification |
| **Accountable executive** | COO |
| **Responsible staff** | DFF (spaces, keys, badges); DIT (equipment, media); supervisors |
| **Approval authority** | DFF (keys/badges); DIT (closet access); COO (camera placement with VPC and Legal) |
| **Documentation** | Key and badge registers; access logs; visitor logs; quarterly reconciliations; incident reports |
| **KPI** | Badge/key discrepancies at reconciliation; unsecured-space reports; devices stolen; sites with locked shredding (target 100%) |
| **Review frequency** | Quarterly |
| **Escalation threshold** | Any unsecured residential site → immediate; lost key → 1 business day rekey assessment; unauthorized person in a restricted space |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** theft, rekeying, breach response. **Legal:** HIPAA physical safeguards. **Operational:** equipment loss at sites. **Resident service:** these are people's homes; a security failure is a dignity and safety failure. **Reputational:** significant. **Cyber:** physical access defeats most technical controls. |

### CR-20 — Security Awareness and Phishing Simulation (SOP-20)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Staff unable to recognize the attacks actually aimed at them; low reporting allowing incidents to run undetected |
| **Minimum control** | Training before account activation and annually; regular simulations using realistic scenarios; report-rate measurement; supportive coaching rather than punishment |
| **Accountable executive** | COO; CHRO co-owns assignment and records |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; HR; supervisors |
| **Approval authority** | COO (program and any consequence framework, with CHRO) |
| **Documentation** | Completion records; campaign results; coaching records; curriculum versions |
| **KPI** | Completion % by department; click rate; **report rate**; median report time; repeat clickers; new hires trained before activation |
| **Review frequency** | Monthly |
| **Escalation threshold** | Department completion below 90% at 45 days; click rate above ⬜ [10%]; report rate below ⬜ [40%] |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** the human layer is where most losses originate. **Legal:** HIPAA training requirement. **Operational:** repeated incident handling consumes IT capacity. **Resident service:** indirect. **Reputational:** "staff were never trained" is indefensible. **Cyber:** the highest-return control per dollar available to this organization. |

### CR-21 — Records Retention and Legal Hold (SOP-21)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Destroying records under a preservation obligation; retaining records long past requirement; inability to produce records on demand |
| **Minimum control** | One legally reviewed retention schedule; Vault configuration matching it; written holds applied technically within 1 business day; hold register; custodian acknowledgments |
| **Accountable executive** | COO; VPC and Legal own retention authority |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; custodians; supervisors |
| **Approval authority** | Legal / VPC (issue and release holds); COO (disposal program) |
| **Documentation** | Retention schedule with version history; hold notices, acknowledgments, releases; hold register; disposal logs |
| **KPI** | Active holds; holds technically verified (target 100%); custodian acknowledgment rate; retention configuration matched to schedule; production requests met on time |
| **Review frequency** | Quarterly (holds); annual (configuration verification) |
| **Escalation threshold** | Hold not applied within 1 business day → COO and Legal; any deletion within a hold scope → Legal immediately; any instruction to delete records |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** sanctions, adverse inference, settlement pressure. **Legal:** spoliation is among the most damaging findings a defendant can face. **Operational:** unbounded storage cost and search burden. **Resident service:** loss of historical service records. **Reputational:** destruction of evidence, however inadvertent, reads as concealment. **Cyber:** excess retained data enlarges every breach. |

### CR-22 — Technology Budget and Lifecycle (SOP-22)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Accumulating technical debt; unpredictable emergency spending; platform decisions made on features rather than total cost |
| **Minimum control** | Approved lifecycle standards; rolling 5-year refresh schedule built from the actual inventory; four-category budget with contingency; TCO and exit cost documented before any material purchase |
| **Accountable executive** | COO; DF co-owns |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT; Finance |
| **Approval authority** | COO (plan); Finance (budget); CEO/Board (capital per policy) |
| **Documentation** | Lifecycle plan; budget submissions and variance; TCO and exit-cost analyses; capital approvals |
| **KPI** | % devices past lifecycle; replacement forecast vs budget; variance to budget; unused-license cost recovered; emergency replacements |
| **Review frequency** | Monthly (variance); annual (plan) |
| **Escalation threshold** | Past-lifecycle above ⬜ [15%]; variance above ⬜ [10%]; any platform decision without a documented exit cost |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** deferred replacement is borrowing at a high implied rate — paid back in downtime, support hours, and emergency purchases. **Legal:** unsupported systems fail compliance requirements. **Operational:** rising failure rates. **Resident service:** aging equipment at sites. **Reputational:** internal, mostly. **Cyber:** unsupported hardware and software cannot be patched. |

### CR-23 — Website, Social Media, and External Digital Communications (SOP-23)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Publishing information about an individual served without consent; orphaned or hijacked accounts; lapsed domain taking down email and website |
| **Minimum control** | Channel register with organizational ownership and MFA; consent verified before any content referencing individuals served; domain and registrar controlled with registry lock and auto-renew; tiered content approval |
| **Accountable executive** | COO; Marketing and Development responsible |
| **Responsible staff** | Marketing; Development; DIT |
| **Approval authority** | Marketing (routine); + VPC and program (content referencing individuals); CEO + Legal (positions, crisis, media) |
| **Documentation** | Channel register and administrator reviews; consent records; content approvals; takedown records |
| **KPI** | Channels with organizational ownership and MFA (target 100%); administrator review completion; consent exceptions (target zero); domain expiration margin; impersonation reports |
| **Review frequency** | Quarterly |
| **Escalation threshold** | Any unauthorized post; any individual identified without consent → VPC immediately; domain within 90 days of expiration; any impersonation or fraudulent donation page |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** fundraising disruption; fraudulent donation diversion. **Legal:** privacy and consent violations; HIPAA where a service relationship is disclosed. **Operational:** a lapsed domain takes down email organization-wide. **Resident service:** dignity and privacy harm. **Reputational:** the most public failure mode in this manual. **Cyber:** hijacked social accounts are used against our own community. |

### CR-24 — Technology Accessibility and Accommodation (SOP-24)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Staff with disabilities unable to perform their work; inaccessible public digital services; accommodation requests stalled in procurement |
| **Minimum control** | Accessibility assessed on every procurement; accommodation implemented within target; accessible training materials; website assessed annually against a stated standard |
| **Accountable executive** | COO; CHRO owns the accommodation process |
| **Responsible staff** | DIT (technical); HR (process); Marketing (public content) |
| **Approval authority** | CHRO (accommodation determination); DIT (technical solution); COO (cost above threshold) |
| **Documentation** | Procurement accessibility assessments; accommodation implementation records (no medical information held by IT); website assessments and remediation plans |
| **KPI** | % procurements with accessibility assessment; median accommodation implementation time; website assessment currency; open accessibility findings |
| **Review frequency** | Annual (program); per request (implementation) |
| **Escalation threshold** | Accommodation not implemented within 10 business days of approval → CHRO and COO; any employee blocked by an inaccessible system; any accessibility complaint |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** retrofit costs and legal exposure. **Legal:** ADA and NJ Law Against Discrimination. **Operational:** capable staff unable to work. **Resident service:** families and individuals unable to reach us. **Reputational:** for an organization serving people with disabilities, this is the most self-contradicting failure available. **Cyber:** workarounds staff invent to use inaccessible systems often break security rules. |

### CR-25 — Business Continuity for Utility and System Loss (SOP-25)
| | |
|---|---|
| **Risk** | Residential and day program services disrupted by loss of power, phone, internet, cloud, or a vendor system |
| **Minimum control** | Per-site backup calling method; paper downtime packets verified quarterly; tested mass-notification channel; generator/UPS coverage documented; semi-annual drills; recovery documentation after every event |
| **Accountable executive** | COO; VPO responsible for program execution |
| **Responsible staff** | Site supervisors; Clinical/Nursing; Facilities; DIT; Finance |
| **Approval authority** | VPO and COO (program decisions, relocation); COO (emergency spend); Clinical (clinical downtime procedures) |
| **Documentation** | Continuity logs; downtime documentation; recovery reconciliation; drill records; quarterly site verification |
| **KPI** | Sites with verified downtime packets (%); drills completed; time to activate downtime procedures; recovery documentation completed on time; mass-notification tests |
| **Review frequency** | Quarterly (site verification); semi-annual (drills) |
| **Escalation threshold** | Any threat to resident safety → immediate; inability to maintain temperature, medical equipment power, or emergency communication → VPO and COO immediately; outage >4 hours at a residential site → CEO |
| **Failure impact** | **Financial:** overtime, emergency services, lost billing days. **Legal:** documentation timeliness and licensing requirements do not pause during an outage. **Operational:** improvised responses across 24/7 sites. **Resident service:** the highest-stakes category — medication, supervision, and emergency response. **Reputational:** severe and immediate. **Cyber:** continuity failures during a cyber incident compound both. |

---
## 4.3 Executive Technology Dashboard

> **⬜ ALL TARGETS BELOW ARE PROPOSED TARGETS REQUIRING LEADERSHIP APPROVAL.** They are starting points calibrated to a multi-site nonprofit with an outsourced IT model — not commitments. Before adoption, each must be (a) approved by the COO, (b) reconciled to the MSP contract where the MSP delivers the service, and (c) tested against two to three months of baseline data. **Publishing a target the organization cannot yet meet damages credibility more than publishing an honest baseline with an improvement trajectory.**

**Format:** one page, monthly, produced by [Director of Information Technology] within 10 business days of month end, reviewed at the Technology Governance Committee and by the CEO. Show current month, prior month, 12-month trend, and target. **A missing number is reported as missing — never as zero.**

### 4.3.1 Service delivery

| # | KPI | Definition | Proposed target | Escalation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open work orders by priority | Count open at month end, split P1–P4 | P1: 0 · P2: <5 · P3: <25 · P4: no limit | Any open P1 |
| 2 | Open work order aging | Oldest open ticket per priority; count beyond target | Zero beyond 2× target | Any ticket at 2× target |
| 3 | Average first-response time | Mean time from submission to named-owner acknowledgement, by priority | P1 ≤15 min · P2 ≤1 bus. hr · P3 ≤4 bus. hr · P4 ≤1 bus. day | Attainment <90% |
| 4 | Average time to resolution | Mean submission to confirmed closure, by priority | P1 ≤4 hr · P2 ≤1 bus. day · P3 ≤3 bus. days · P4 ≤10 bus. days | Attainment <85% |
| 5 | SLA attainment % | % of tickets meeting both response and resolution targets | ≥90% overall; ≥95% for P1–P2 | <85% two consecutive months |
| 6 | Repeat incidents | Assets, sites, vendors, or issue types with 3+ tickets in 90 days | Declining trend; <10 active flags | Any flag open >90 days without a decision |
| 7 | Ticket quality | % of tickets with valid site, asset, impact statement, and recorded root cause | ≥90% | <75% |
| 8 | Requester satisfaction | Mean score on closure survey | ≥4.2 / 5.0 with ≥30% response rate | <3.8 |

### 4.3.2 Availability and continuity

| # | KPI | Definition | Proposed target | Escalation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 9 | Internet downtime by site | Total minutes unavailable per site per month | ≤120 min/site/month; ≤60 for residential | Any residential site >120 min |
| 10 | Phone downtime by site | Total minutes unavailable per site per month | ≤60 min/site/month | Any site with no working phone >30 min |
| 11 | Sites with repeat outages | Sites with 3+ outages in 90 days | Zero | Any site reaching 3 |
| 12 | Critical incident count | SEV 1 and SEV 2 incidents | SEV 1: 0 · SEV 2: ≤1/quarter | Any SEV 1 |
| 13 | Mean time to restore (major) | Declaration to verified restoration | ≤4 hours | >8 hours |
| 14 | Backup success rate | % of scheduled jobs completing successfully | ≥99% | <97% or any 48-hour unresolved failure |
| 15 | Backup restore testing | Restore tests completed and passed this quarter | 100% of scheduled tests passed | Any failed test |
| 16 | Downtime readiness | % of sites with verified current downtime packets and a tested backup calling method | 100% | <95% |

### 4.3.3 Security and access

| # | KPI | Definition | Proposed target | Escalation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 17 | Phishing reports | Suspicious messages reported by staff per month | Trend upward; ≥ ⬜ [set baseline] | Sudden decline (suggests under-reporting) |
| 18 | Phishing simulation click rate | % clicking in simulations | ≤5% (interim ≤10%) | >10% |
| 19 | Phishing simulation report rate | % reporting the simulation | ≥60% (interim ≥40%) | <40% |
| 20 | Security training completion | % of assigned staff completing on time | ≥95% | <90%, or any department <80% |
| 21 | MFA coverage | % of accounts with MFA on email, PHI, finance, payroll, HR systems | 100% | <100% |
| 22 | User access review completion | % of supervisors certifying by deadline | 100% | <90% at deadline |
| 23 | Offboarding within timeframe | % of separations with access disabled within required time | 100% involuntary; ≥98% overall | Any involuntary miss; overall <95% |
| 24 | Orphaned / dormant accounts | Active accounts with no owner or no sign-in in 60 days | Zero | Any |
| 25 | Privileged account count | Total privileged accounts, with trend | Flat or declining; each justified | Any unexplained increase |
| 26 | Median time to report an incident | Detection by staff to report | ≤30 minutes | >2 hours |
| 27 | Median time to contain | Report to containment | ≤1 hour for SEV 1–2 | >4 hours |
| 28 | Payment fraud attempts detected | BEC and banking-change attempts caught by verification | All attempts caught; zero losses | Any loss |
| 29 | Security exceptions open | Active approved exceptions, with age | ≤5 open; none past expiration | Any expired exception still in effect |
| 30 | Overdue remediation items | Corrective actions past due date | Zero | Any item >60 days overdue |

### 4.3.4 Assets, software, and vendors

|---|---|---|---|---|
| 31 | Devices inventoried | % of known devices in the single inventory with complete records | 100% | <98% |
| 32 | Devices encrypted | % of data-capable devices encrypted | 100% | <100% |

| 42 | Vendor renewals reviewed on time | % reviewed at 90 days before renewal | 100% | Any auto-renewal without review |

| 44 | AI training completion | % of AI tool users trained before access and annually | 100% before access; ≥95% annual | Any untrained user with access |
| 45 | AI incidents | Reported errors, harmful outputs, privacy concerns, unauthorized use | Trend visible; zero PHI entries | Any PHI entered into AI |
| 46 | Unauthorized AI tools found | Per quarterly discovery | Declining | Any tool processing confidential data |
| 47 | AI spend vs benefit | Annual AI cost against documented outcomes | Every dollar tied to a named decision improved | Any tool unused for 90 days |

### 4.3.6 Financial and governance

| # | KPI | Definition | Proposed target | Escalation |

| 49 | Cost per user | Total technology operating cost ÷ active users | Tracked with trend; benchmarked annually | Increase >⬜ [10%] YoY without an explained cause |
| 50 | Downtime cost estimate | Estimated labor and revenue impact of outages | Declining | Any month exceeding ⬜ [INSERT $] |
| 51 | Policy exceptions open | Active exceptions by risk level | ≤5; none expired | Any high/critical exception past expiration |
| 52 | Audit and survey findings | Technology-related findings from any audit, survey, or funder review | Zero repeat findings | Any repeat finding |

### 4.3.7 Recommended one-page executive summary layout

```
TECHNOLOGY DASHBOARD — [MONTH YEAR]                    Prepared by: [DIT]

┌─ RED / AMBER ITEMS REQUIRING A DECISION ────────────────────────────┐
│ 1. [Item] — [number vs target] — [decision needed] — [owner] [date] │
│ 2. ...                                                              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

SERVICE          Open P1: _  P2: _  | SLA attainment: __% | Satisfaction: _._
AVAILABILITY     Site downtime (worst 3): ____________ | SEV1: _ SEV2: _
SECURITY         MFA: __% | Training: __% | Report rate: __% | Offboarding: __%
ACCESS           Review completion: __% | Orphaned: _ | Privileged: _ (Δ _)
ASSETS           Inventoried __% | Encrypted __% | Past lifecycle __%
SOFTWARE         Utilization __% | Unused license cost $______
VENDOR / MSP     Scorecard: __/100 | SLA attainment __%
AI               Tools: _ | Trained: __% | Incidents: _
FINANCIAL        Spend vs budget: ±__% | Downtime cost est: $______
OPEN ITEMS       Exceptions: _ | Overdue remediation: _ | Repeat issues: _

TREND NOTE: [two sentences on what moved and why]
ASK: [the one decision or resource needed from leadership this month]
```

> **A note on dashboard discipline.** Fifty-two metrics is the full instrument panel; it is not what goes in front of the CEO monthly. The one-page summary above is the executive view, and the full register is the working document behind it. If a metric has not driven a decision in twelve months, retire it — a dashboard nobody acts on is overhead, not instrumentation.

# PART V — STAFF QUICK REFERENCE

> **Print these. Post them.** Each guide below is designed to fit on one page. Post QR-1 through QR-6 at every site, every shared workstation, and every phone. During an outage, the printed copy is the only copy that works.

---

## QR-1 — REPORTING AN IT ISSUE

**Something is broken. What do I do?**

**STEP 1 — Is anyone in danger?**
→ **YES:** Call **911**. Then [Emergency Contact Number]. Then tell your supervisor.
→ **NO:** Continue.

**STEP 2 — Is this a security problem?** (Strange message, clicked a bad link, files locked, device lost)
→ **YES:** **Call [IT Support Phone] NOW.** After hours: **[Emergency IT Contact]**. See QR-3 and QR-4.
→ **NO:** Continue.

**STEP 3 — Try the two-minute checks.**
- Printer → QR guide in SOP-04 · Phone → QR guide in SOP-05 · Internet → QR-6
- Restart your own device. Never restart network equipment.

**STEP 4 — Still broken? Submit a work order.**
→ **[Work Order Submission Link]**

**STEP 5 — Is it urgent?** (Site can't operate · only device at the site · deadline today · resident services affected)
→ **YES:** Mark it **High** or **Emergency** AND **call [IT Support Phone]** with your ticket number.

**STEP 6 — Write down your ticket number.**

| Need | Contact |
|---|---|
| Submit a work order | **[Work Order Submission Link]** |
| IT support | [IT Support Phone] · [IT Support Email] |
| After-hours emergency IT | [Emergency IT Contact] |
| Program on-call | [Emergency Contact Number] |
| Life safety | **911** |

---

## QR-2 — SUBMITTING A WORK ORDER

### 👉 **[WORK ORDER SUBMISSION LINK]**
*One link for IT, Facilities, and Fleet.*

**Have this ready before you start:**

| ☐ | Information | Example |
|---|---|---|
| ☐ | Your name, title, phone that will be answered | "Cell: 555-0100" |
| ☐ | **Site** and exact location | "Group Home 7 — kitchen" |
| ☐ | **Asset tag / serial / extension / vehicle number** | "IT-PRN-0142" or "Ext. 412" or "Unit 214" |
| ☐ | **Exact error message** (photograph it) | "Paper Jam – Area 3" |
| ☐ | When it started; has it happened before? | "6:00 AM today; third time this month" |
| ☐ | Who is affected and what work is blocked | "6 staff; mailing due 4 PM" |
| ☐ | **Does this affect resident services or safety?** | Yes / No |
| ☐ | Photo attached | ✅ |
| ☐ | Access times and instructions | "24/7; call [number] before arrival" |

**Choose the right priority:**

| Priority | Use when | Also do this |
|---|---|---|
| **EMERGENCY** | Resident safety · site can't operate · no way to call for help · suspected hacking | **CALL [IT Support Phone] immediately** |
| **HIGH** | A program function is down · multiple staff blocked · deadline today | Call if urgent |
| **NORMAL** | One person blocked · routine request | Submit and wait |
| **LOW** | Convenience · future request | Submit and wait |

**Don't wait for a work order — pick up the phone — if:** someone could be harmed, data could be lost, a site has no phone or no internet, or you think you've been hacked.

---

## QR-3 — RECOGNIZING AND REPORTING PHISHING

**Look for these:**

| 🚩 | Red flag |
|---|---|
| 1 | **Urgency** — "today," "immediately," "before I go into a meeting" |
| 2 | A request for **money, gift cards, banking changes, or payroll data** |
| 3 | A request to **log in** through a link |
| 4 | Sender address doesn't match the display name, or is slightly off |
| 5 | **External banner** on a message that claims to be internal |
| 6 | An **attachment you weren't expecting** |
| 7 | "Keep this confidential" or "don't call me" |
| 8 | Poor grammar, odd phrasing, or a generic greeting |
| 9 | A link whose real destination (hover to see) doesn't match the text |
| 10 | A **new phone number** claiming to be an executive |

**What to do — in this order:**
1. **STOP.** Don't click. Don't reply. Don't open attachments. Don't forward to coworkers.
2. **REPORT** — Gmail three-dot menu → **Report phishing** — AND forward to ⬜ [PHISHING REPORT ADDRESS].
3. **If it's about money or an executive** → also call [IT Support Phone] and [Director of Finance].
4. **Wait** for IT to confirm before deleting.

### ⚠️ If you already clicked, replied, or entered your password:
1. **Call [IT Support Phone] immediately.** After hours: [Emergency IT Contact].
2. Disconnect from the network.
3. Don't change your password yourself — IT will do it together with revoking the attacker's access.
4. Tell your supervisor.
5. **You will not be in trouble.** Reporting fast is what protects everyone.

**Remember:** No executive at [Organization Name] will ever ask you by email or text to buy gift cards, send a wire, or share employee data.

---

## QR-4 — SUSPECTED RANSOMWARE OR MALWARE

> # 🛑 STOP · DISCONNECT · REPORT

**Signs:** files renamed or won't open · a ransom note on screen · sudden extreme slowness · programs opening by themselves · security software disabled · network drives suddenly erroring.

**Do this, in order:**

1. **STOP.** Stop typing. Don't click anything else.
2. **DISCONNECT.** Unplug the network cable. Turn off Wi-Fi.
3. **DO NOT turn the computer off** *(unless there's smoke, fire, or a safety hazard)*. Powering off destroys evidence we need.
4. **PHOTOGRAPH the screen** with your phone.
5. **CALL [IT Support Phone].** After hours: **[Emergency IT Contact]**. Do not email — email may be affected.
6. **TELL your supervisor and site lead.**
7. **WARN colleagues verbally** — tell them not to use shared drives until IT says it's safe.
8. **TELL IT if shared drive files are affected** — that means it's spreading, and it changes everything about the response.
9. **DON'T** run a virus scan, delete files, try to clean it up, or plug in a USB drive or phone.
10. **STAY AVAILABLE** — IT will need to know what you were doing right before.

**If the device is needed for resident care:** tell IT immediately so a replacement is prioritized. Do not keep using the suspect device.

---

## QR-5 — PASSWORD RESET OR LOST MFA DEVICE

### Locked out or forgot your password

1. **Try self-service:** ⬜ [SELF-SERVICE RESET URL]
2. **If that doesn't work → CALL [IT Support Phone].** After hours: [Emergency IT Contact].
3. **Have ready:** your name, title, department, site, supervisor's name, the system affected, the exact error.
4. **Expect identity verification.** IT will confirm who you are before resetting anything. If they can't confirm it, they won't reset it — that's the rule protecting your account.
5. **You'll get a temporary password** by phone or to the number already on file — never by email.
6. **Sign in quickly** (it expires) and **set a new password immediately.**
7. **Confirm** to IT that you have access.

> ### ⛔ IT will NEVER reset a password from an email request alone. IT will NEVER ask you for your password. Never share yours with anyone — including your supervisor.

### Account locked out?
1. Wait ⬜ [15–30] minutes — many clear on their own.
2. Check **Caps Lock**.
3. **Check your other devices** — an old saved password on a phone or tablet retrying in the background is the #1 cause. Update or remove it.
4. Still locked → call [IT Support Phone].

### Lost, stolen, broken, or replaced phone with your MFA app

1. **Call [IT Support Phone] immediately** — day or night. Don't wait for your next shift.
2. If the phone also had organizational email or files → say so; that's a lost-device report too.
3. IT will verify your identity (more strictly than usual — this is a favorite attacker trick), remove the old device, and help you re-enroll.
4. **Getting a new phone?** Submit a work order **before** you wipe or trade in the old one.
5. **Store your backup codes** in the password manager or a locked place — never in email, never on the same phone.

> **MFA is never turned off to get you back in.** You get back in by re-enrolling.

---

## QR-6 — RESPONDING TO AN INTERNET OUTAGE

**FIRST — safety:**
☐ Can staff still call **911** and the **on-call nurse/manager**? If NO → **call [Emergency IT Contact] now**, notify the on-call manager, and deploy cell phones.
☐ Are fire alarm, emergency call, or door security systems affected? → Follow site emergency procedures.

**THEN — find out how big it is:**

| Question | If YES |
|---|---|
| Is it only my device? | Restart my device → submit a work order (Normal) |
| Is it only Wi-Fi (wired still works)? | Use wired → submit a work order |
| Is the whole site down? | **Call [IT Support Phone]** — residential sites call immediately |
| Are other sites down too? | Tell IT — this is organization-wide |

**ALWAYS:**
1. **Change nothing.** Do not unplug, reset, or reconfigure any router, modem, switch, or access point. Photograph the lights instead.
2. **Log the start time.**
3. **Switch to paper downtime forms** for documentation, medication records, attendance, and incident reports.
4. **Tell staff on shift** what's happening and what the backup calling method is.
5. **Notify** the on-call manager (residential sites) and your supervisor.
6. **Record what couldn't be completed**, so it can be entered when service returns.
7. **Ask IT about a hotspot** if the outage passes ⬜ [1 hour] or blocks clinical documentation.

**When it comes back:** test a website, email, the clinical system, and the phones (in AND out) before telling staff it's fixed. Log the end time. Enter your paper documentation within ⬜ [INSERT] hours.

---

## QR-7 — USING AI SAFELY

### ✅ You MAY use an approved AI tool to:
draft and improve emails · outline reports and plans · summarize your own notes · draft job descriptions and scopes of work · simplify language · brainstorm options · draft checklists and templates

### ❌ You may NEVER:
1. Enter **anything about a person we serve** — no names, no initials, no descriptions, no dates, no diagnoses, no incidents.
2. Enter **employee personnel information** — reviews, discipline, medical, pay.
3. Enter **SSNs, account numbers, passwords, or financial data.**
4. Use AI to **decide** anything about someone's services, housing, health, benefits, job, discipline, or money.
5. Use a **personal AI account** or a tool that isn't on the **[Approved AI Tool List]**.
6. **Record or transcribe** a meeting with AI without everyone's consent and IT approval.
7. **Publish AI output you haven't verified.**

### Before you type — 5 checks
> 1. ☐ Is this tool on the **[Approved AI Tool List]**?
> 2. ☐ Am I signed in with my **organizational account**?
> 3. ☐ Could anything I'm entering **identify a real person**?
> 4. ☐ Is this a **decision about a person**? → Stop.
> 5. ☐ Can I **verify every fact** it gives me?

### After it answers
- **Open every citation.** AI invents regulations, statistics, and quotes that look completely real.
- **Check every number** against its actual source.
- **Read for dignity** — watch for language that diminishes people with disabilities.
- **You own it.** "The AI wrote it" is not a defense.

**Made a mistake, or saw something wrong?** Report to [IT Support Email]. If you entered personal information → **call [IT Support Phone] right away.** You will not be in trouble for reporting.

---

## QR-8 — EMAIL BEST PRACTICES

### Before you send — 8 checks
> 1. ☐ **Recipients** — did autocomplete pick the wrong person?
> 2. ☐ **Reply All** — does everyone really need this?
> 3. ☐ **Confidential content** — PHI, personnel, donor, or financial data? Is this the approved secure method?
> 4. ☐ **Attachments** — right file? Hidden tabs checked? Should it be a link instead?
> 5. ☐ **Link permissions** — restricted to the right people?
> 6. ☐ **Subject line** — topic + action + date?
> 7. ☐ **Tone** — comfortable if a surveyor, attorney, funder, or the Board reads it?
> 8. ☐ **Money or access** — does this request or approve a payment or an access change? Verification done?

### Subject line format
`[ACTION | DECISION NEEDED | FYI | URGENT] – Topic – By Date`
> `ACTION – FY26 Facilities Budget Worksheet – Return by 10/3`

### Quick rules
| Do | Don't |
|---|---|
| Use your organizational account for all work | Use personal email for organizational business |
| Put the ask in the first two sentences | Bury the request at the bottom |
| Use CC openly and say why | Use BCC to secretly copy someone |
| Send a link from [Approved File-Sharing Platform] | Attach files with confidential information |
| Route media, legal, and regulatory mail to the right office immediately | Answer a reporter, attorney, or regulator yourself |
| Set an out-of-office naming a covering colleague | Say you're traveling or out of the country |
| Report phishing within 1 hour | Forward suspicious mail to coworkers |
| Verify money requests by phone | Act on emailed payment instructions |

### Sent it to the wrong person?
**Don't try to fix it quietly.** Tell your supervisor → report to [IT Support Email] and Compliance **within 1 hour** → don't delete the sent message.

```
ANNUAL ACKNOWLEDGEMENT — INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY, COMMUNICATIONS,
CYBERSECURITY, AND ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE GOVERNANCE SOP MANUAL

Title: _____________________________  Department: _______________________

     [Work Order Submission Link], and must call for emergencies.

     banking information without the required verification.
10. ☐ I understand that organizational systems, devices, accounts, and networks
     are the property of [Organization Name]; that I should have no expectation
     of privacy in information created, stored, sent, or received on them,
     subject to applicable law; and that the organization may access, monitor,
     review, retain, and disclose that information for legitimate business
     purposes.
11. ☐ I understand I must return all organizational property, records, keys,
     badges, and devices on request or at separation, and that my
     confidentiality obligations continue after my employment ends.
12. ☐ I understand that failure to follow these requirements may result in
     disciplinary action up to and including termination, and that certain
     violations may carry legal consequences.
13. ☐ I have completed the required training for this period, or I have a
     scheduled date to do so: ______

I understand this acknowledgement does not create a contract of employment or
alter my at-will employment status.
```

## APPENDIX K — EMAIL SIGNATURE STANDARD

**Standard signature — use exactly this structure.** ⬜ *[Confirm fonts, colors, logo, and any required disclaimer with Marketing and Legal before deployment.]*

```
[First Last], [Credentials if applicable]
[Job Title]
[Organization Name]
[Street Address] | [City, NJ ZIP]
Office: [phone] | Mobile: [phone, if appropriate to share]
[email] | [website]

[Logo image — max ⬜ 150px wide]

[Optional mission line, one sentence, approved by Marketing]
```

**Rules:**
1. **Do** use the same signature on every message, including replies ⬜ *(or a shortened version on replies — leadership decision)*.
2. **Do not** add personal quotes, images, political or religious statements, seasonal graphics, or personal social media links.
3. **Do not** include a mobile number if your role does not warrant being reachable that way.
4. **Do not** add a "sent from my phone" line in place of the standard signature.
5. **Do** include credentials only where they are accurate, current, and relevant.
6. **Confidentiality notice** ⬜ **[LEGAL DECISION — a footer disclaimer has limited legal effect and adds length to every message. Recommend a short version or none. If adopted:]**
   > *This message and any attachments are confidential and intended only for the named recipient. If you received this in error, please notify the sender and delete it. Unauthorized use or disclosure is prohibited.*
7. **Pronouns** may be included at the individual's option ⬜ *[confirm organizational position with HR]*.
8. **Accessibility:** logo images must carry alt text; do not convey information only through an image.
9. **Deployment:** IT applies the signature centrally where possible so it stays consistent and updates automatically when titles change.

---

## APPENDIX L — EMAIL TEMPLATES

### L.1 Standard business email
```
Subject: [ACTION | DECISION NEEDED | FYI] – [Topic] – [By Date]

[Name],

[One sentence: what this is about.]
[One sentence: what I need from you and by when.]

Background:
1. [Fact]
2. [Fact]

Options / Recommendation:
- Option A: [description] — [cost/impact]
- Option B: [description] — [cost/impact]
- Recommendation: [choice and one-line reason]

Next step: [who does what by when]

Thank you,
[Signature per Appendix K]
```

### L.2 Out-of-office (internal)
```
Thank you for your message. I am out of the office from [start] and will
return on [return date]. I will respond when I return.

For immediate assistance, contact [Name, Title] at [email] or [phone].
For urgent site or resident matters, contact [Program/On-Call Line] at
[Emergency Contact Number].
For IT support: [Work Order Submission Link] or [IT Support Phone].

[Name] · [Title] · [Organization Name]
```

### L.3 Out-of-office (external — shorter, less specific)
```
Thank you for your message. I am out of the office and will respond when I
return on [date]. For immediate assistance, please contact [Name] at [email]
or [phone].

[Name] · [Title] · [Organization Name]
```

### L.4 Holding reply
```
[Name],

Received — thank you. I am reviewing this and will respond by [date/time]
with [what you will provide].

[Signature]
```

### L.5 Departed employee autoresponder
```
Thank you for your message. [Name] is no longer with [Organization Name].
Please direct [subject area] inquiries to [Name, Title] at [email] or
[phone]. Your message has been forwarded to the appropriate team member.
```

### L.6 Routing a media inquiry (reply to the reporter)
```
Thank you for reaching out. I am routing your request to our communications
office, who will respond to you directly.

[Signature]
```
*Then forward the original message immediately to [Marketing/Communications Contact] and [Executive Director/CEO], unanswered.*

### L.7 Legal hold notice *(issued by Legal or Compliance only)*
```
Subject: LEGAL HOLD NOTICE — DO NOT DELETE — [Matter Name]

[Name],

[Organization Name] is required to preserve records related to [brief
description of the matter]. You have been identified as a custodian of
potentially relevant records.

EFFECTIVE IMMEDIATELY, you must preserve and must NOT delete, alter,
discard, or destroy any of the following, in any format:

  • Email, calendar entries, and attachments
  • Documents, spreadsheets, and presentations, including drafts
  • Text messages, chat messages, and voicemail
  • Photographs, video, and audio recordings
  • Paper records and handwritten notes
  • Records in [list specific systems]

Date range: [start] to [present / end]
Subject matter: [description]

This hold suspends all routine deletion and retention schedules for these
records. It remains in effect until you receive written notice from me
releasing it. Do not discuss this matter with anyone other than [issuer]
and [Legal Counsel Contact].

Please confirm receipt by replying to this message within 3 business days.

Questions: [issuer name, title, phone].

[Issuer] · [Title]
```

## APPENDIX M — INTERNET OUTAGE COMMUNICATION TEMPLATES

### M.1 Initial staff notice
```
Subject: [SITE/ALL SITES] Internet Service Disruption — [Time]

We are experiencing an internet service disruption affecting [site(s)],
beginning at approximately [time].

What is affected: [email / clinical documentation / billing / phones / other]
What still works: [phones / cellular / paper processes]

What to do now:
1. Switch to paper downtime procedures for [functions].
2. Record everything you cannot enter; you will enter it after restoration.
3. Do not change any network equipment settings.
4. For emergencies, use [backup calling method]. 911 is available by [method].

Next update: [time — no later than 30 minutes from now].
Questions: [IT Support Phone].

[Name, Title]
```

### M.2 Site / program team notice (residential)
```
[Site Name] — Internet is down as of [time].

1. Confirm every staff member on shift can reach 911 and the on-call nurse.
   Backup method for this site: [method].
2. Begin paper documentation now — packets are located [location].
3. Medication documentation: follow the paper procedure in your downtime packet.
4. Resident care and supervision continue as normal.
5. Log the time the outage started: ________
6. Call [IT Support Phone] with any safety concern immediately.

Supervisor on duty: __________   On-call manager notified at: ________
```

### M.3 Vendor notice
```
Subject: Service Disruption Notice — [Organization Name]

We are currently experiencing an internet service disruption affecting
[scope], beginning [date/time]. Our systems may be unreachable and our
response times may be delayed.

This is not an issue with your service. We will notify you when normal
operations resume. Urgent matters: [phone].

[Name, Title]
```

### M.4 Leadership update
```
Subject: OUTAGE UPDATE [#] — [Scope] — [Time]

Status: [Investigating / Identified / Restoring / Monitoring / Resolved]
Started: [time]   Duration so far: [x hours y minutes]
Sites affected: [list]   Staff affected: [number]

Cause: [known / under investigation / ISP confirmed outage — ref #____]
Current ETA: [time or "not yet available from carrier"]

Operational impact:
  • Resident safety: [status — emergency communication confirmed at all sites?]
  • Clinical documentation: [paper in use / at risk]
  • Billing/payroll: [status, deadlines at risk]
  • Other: [detail]

Actions taken: [1, 2, 3]
Actions in progress: [1, 2, 3]
Decisions needed from leadership: [or "none at this time"]

Next update: [time]
[Name, Title]
```

### M.5 Restoration notice
```
Subject: RESOLVED — Internet Service Restored — [Site(s)]

Internet service was restored at [time]. Total duration: [x hours].

Verified working: websites, email, [clinical system], phones (inbound and
outbound), printing, [other].

What you need to do now:
1. Enter documentation recorded on paper during the outage, within [X] hours.
2. Report anything still not working to [Work Order Submission Link].
3. Site leads: log the restoration time and return downtime packets to storage.

Cause: [brief]. A root-cause review will be completed by [date].

[Name, Title]
```

### M.6 Extended outage notice
```
Subject: EXTENDED OUTAGE — [Site(s)] — Expected duration [estimate]

The outage that began at [time] is now expected to continue until
approximately [estimate]. [Reason.]

We are taking these steps:
1. [Hotspot deployed / alternate work location authorized / staffing adjusted]
2. [Priority given to: emergency communication, clinical documentation, payroll]
3. [Carrier escalation: ticket #____, escalated to ____ at ____]

For staff at affected sites: continue paper procedures. [Specific instructions.]
For administrative staff: [alternate location / remote work guidance].

Next update: [time]. Questions: [IT Support Phone].
[Name, Title]
```

> ⚠️ **All external communication requires [Executive Director/CEO] and [Legal Counsel Contact] approval before release.** These are drafting aids, not pre-approved statements. Do not send anything below without reading SOP-08 I.8 and SOP-17.

### N.1 Internal alert — active threat, act now
```
Subject: URGENT — SECURITY ALERT — ACTION REQUIRED

A security threat is active. Please do the following immediately:

1. [Specific action — e.g., "Do not open any email with the subject
   '[subject]'. Do not click any link in it."]
2. [Specific action — e.g., "Do not use shared drives until further notice."]
3. If you have already [clicked / entered credentials / opened the attachment]:
   CALL [IT Support Phone] IMMEDIATELY. You will not be in trouble.
4. Report anything suspicious to [IT Support Phone].

Do not forward this message outside the organization.
Next update: [time].

[Director of IT / COO]
```

```

Status: [Contained / Under investigation / Restoring]
What happened: [plain language, factual, no speculation]

What is NOT affected: [be specific — this reduces rumor]

What you need to do:
1. [Action]
2. [Action]

What we are doing: [1, 2, 3]
Next update: [time]

Please do not discuss this outside the organization or on social media.
Media inquiries go to [Marketing/Communications Contact].

[COO]
```

```
WHAT HAPPENED
[Two to four factual sentences. No speculation about cause or attribution.]

CURRENT STATUS
Containment: [complete / in progress]
Services affected: [list]  Services restored: [list]
Resident services impact: [specific]

DATA
Types potentially involved: [PHI / employee / donor / financial / none known]
Individuals potentially affected: [number or "under assessment"]
Breach determination: [pending / not a breach / breach — by Compliance]

FINANCIAL
Direct loss to date: $[____]  Response cost to date: $[____]
Insurance: [carrier notified date; claim # if filed]

NOTIFICATIONS MADE
[Carrier / Legal / Compliance / regulators / law enforcement / affected parties]

NEXT STEPS AND TIMING
1. [Action — owner — date]
2. [Action — owner — date]

Prepared by: [COO]   Next update: [date/time]
This communication is confidential and may be privileged.
```

### N.4 Vendor / partner notification *(Legal approval required)*
```
Subject: Security Notice — [Organization Name]

We are writing to inform you of a security incident that [may have / did]
affect information exchanged between our organizations.

What occurred: [approved factual description]
When: [date range]
Information potentially involved: [description]
What we have done: [containment and remediation summary]
What we recommend you do: [specific actions]

Our point of contact for this matter is [Name, Title, phone, email].

[Executive Director/CEO]
```

### N.5 Affected-individual notification *(HIPAA/NJ content — Legal and Compliance draft and approve; this is a structure only)*
```
[Date]
[Name]
[Address]

Dear [Name],

We are writing to notify you of an incident that may have involved your
personal information.

WHAT HAPPENED: [date discovered, date occurred, plain description]
WHAT INFORMATION WAS INVOLVED: [specific data elements]
WHAT WE ARE DOING: [containment, remediation, safeguards added]
WHAT YOU CAN DO: [specific steps, credit monitoring if offered, how to enroll]
FOR MORE INFORMATION: [dedicated phone number, hours, mailing address]

We take the protection of your information seriously and we regret that this
occurred.

Sincerely,
[Executive Director/CEO]

⬜ REQUIRED ELEMENTS TO CONFIRM WITH LEGAL: HIPAA §164.404 content
   requirements; NJ N.J.S.A. 56:8-163 requirements including notification to
   the NJ State Police Division; timing (HIPAA: without unreasonable delay,
   no later than 60 days); media notice if 500+ individuals in a
   state/jurisdiction; HHS OCR notification timing.
```

### N.6 Holding statement for media *(CEO and Legal approval required before any use)*
```
"[Organization Name] recently identified a [security incident / technology
disruption] affecting certain of our systems. We took immediate steps to
secure our environment and engaged outside experts to assist. Our services to
the individuals we support have continued [without interruption / with the
following adjustments]. We are conducting a thorough review and will provide
notification to anyone affected as required. We have no further details to
share while the review is ongoing."

Spokesperson: [Executive Director/CEO] only, or [Marketing Contact] as
designated. No other employee speaks to media. Refer all inquiries to
[Marketing/Communications Contact].
```

# PART VII — LEADERSHIP ADOPTION CHECKLIST

> **This manual is a draft until this checklist is complete.** Nothing here is optional: every line is either a decision only leadership can make, a value only the organization can supply, or a review only a qualified function can perform. Appendix O carries the placeholder values; this part carries the decisions.

---

## 7.1 Sequencing — recommended rollout

Adopting all 25 SOPs simultaneously will fail. The controls with the highest risk reduction per unit of organizational effort come first, and the peer-dependent elements are introduced only after co-ownership is secured.

| Phase | Weeks | Focus | Why this order |
|---|---|---|---|
| **0 — Baseline** | 1–4 | Site roster · asset inventory · access matrix · current-state assessment of shared accounts, MFA coverage, and backups | You cannot set targets without a baseline, and every later phase depends on the site roster |
| **1 — Front door** | 3–8 | SOP-03 work orders live · SOP-01 email · SOP-02 password/MFA · QR cards printed and posted | These deliver visible benefit to staff first. The manual should arrive as *"support got easier"* before it arrives as *"here are new rules"* |
| **2 — Highest risk** | 6–14 | SOP-08 cybersecurity · SOP-09 access lifecycle · SOP-15 backups · SOP-20 training and simulations | Ransomware, BEC, and retained access are the three exposures that can materially harm the organization |
| **3 — Continuity** | 10–18 | SOP-05 · SOP-06 · SOP-25 · downtime packets · drills | Highest resident-safety value; requires Operations and Clinical co-authorship, so it needs Phase 1 credibility first |
| **4 — Governance and cost** | 14–26 | SOP-10 AI · SOP-11–14 · SOP-16 vendors · SOP-18 change · SOP-22 budget and lifecycle | Where the compounding savings and the platform decisions live |
| **5 — Completion** | 20–30 | SOP-19 · SOP-21 · SOP-23 · SOP-24 · full dashboard · annual cycle established | Completes the set and locks in the review rhythm |

> **Adoption risk worth naming.** This manual asks for cooperation from HR, Compliance, Clinical, Nursing, and program operations. Issued as a mandate from a newly created executive office, it can read as centralization — particularly to leaders whose reporting relationships changed recently. Issued as shared controls with visible peer co-signature and a Phase 1 that makes staff's daily work easier, it reads as service. **The sequencing above is a political design as much as an operational one.** Secure the co-signatures in Section 6.2 before Phase 2, not after.

---

## 7.5 Assumptions and identified gaps

**Stated assumptions** — correct any that are wrong before adoption:
1. The organization uses Google Workspace as its email and file platform.
2. An external managed service provider delivers day-to-day IT support.
3. A single work order system will serve IT, Facilities, and Fleet.
4. Residential sites operate 24/7 and depend on network connectivity for clinical documentation.
5. HIPAA applies to the organization as a covered entity.
6. Cyber liability insurance is in force; its conditions are not yet reflected in this manual.
7. HR, Compliance, and Clinical are peer functions whose cooperation is required, not directed.
8. Retention periods, monitoring language, and enforcement provisions are drafts pending Legal and HR review.

**Known gaps this manual does not close:**
1. **No baseline data.** Every target is a proposal until two to three months of measurement exist.
2. **MSP contract terms are unknown here.** Internal SLAs may exceed what is contracted. Reconcile before publishing.
3. **The access matrix does not exist yet** and cannot be written without each system owner. Until it exists, SOP-09 is manual.
4. **Shared and generic accounts at 24/7 sites** are likely widespread. The remediation effort has not been sized.
5. **Clinical downtime procedures** must be authored by Clinical and Nursing; this manual references them but cannot write them.
6. **Retention periods are unverified** against HIPAA, CMS, DDD, Medicaid, employment, tax, grant, and donor requirements.
7. **Cyber insurance conditions** may impose requirements stricter than this manual, including notification deadlines and mandated vendors.
8. **Physical network equipment locations at residential sites** have not been assessed; some may not be securable without a compensating control.
9. **Embedded AI features** already present in existing applications have not been inventoried.
10. **Accessibility of current systems** has not been assessed; there may be existing accommodation obligations already unmet.
11. **Collective bargaining obligations**, if any, may affect monitoring, discipline, and training-based access suspension.
12. **Budget impact is not quantified here.** SOP-22 defines the method; the numbers require the asset inventory to exist first.
